**Context**: the frontend half of the audit described in D83 — natural-language command safety (workstream 2 of the brief): commands, questions about commands, negation, ambiguity, typos, and adversarial wording, with an explicit requirement to confirm explanatory language never executes and that sensitive mutations still require proposal/approval. `frontend/src/commands/registry.ts` already had 69 passing tests covering ordinary-conversation false-positive avoidance, blocked mutations, and the safe/confirm action tables' basic matching — this audit specifically probed the categories not yet covered by name.

### Risk register (frontend), ranked by severity × likelihood

| # | Finding | Severity | Likelihood | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A **question about a command** ("what does sync calendar do") resolved identically to the command itself — `parseCommand` matches `CONFIRM_ACTIONS`/`SAFE_ACTIONS` by keyword *presence*, regardless of grammar, so a `safe_action` (e.g. a real Google Calendar sync) executed immediately purely from being asked what it does. | **High** for `safe_action` matches — an immediate real network call from idle curiosity, with no refusal cue of any kind for a human reader either | Medium (asking what a voice command does is a very natural way to explore the interface) | **Fixed** |
| 2 | **Negated phrasing** ("don't disconnect google calendar", "never sync health") resolved identically to its affirmative form for the same reason. | Medium-High for `safe_action` (an unwanted real action executes with zero confirmation despite explicit refusal); Low for `confirm_required` (a real `ConfirmDialog` still gates actual harm — the worst outcome is an unwanted dialog the user cancels) | Medium (a natural way to phrase caution or correct oneself mid-sentence) | **Fixed** |
| 3 | **Ambiguity** (a bare "sync" or "disconnect it" with no named provider) — verified already safe by construction: `SAFE_ACTIONS`/`CONFIRM_ACTIONS` require both a provider keyword (`calendar`/`health`) and an action keyword before matching, so an ambiguous phrase naming no provider falls through to `none` (sent to Jarvis as an ordinary question) rather than guessing. | Low | N/A | **Confirmed safe, no defect** |
| 4 | **Typos** ("expor data", "discconect calendar") — verified already safe by construction: every `requires` entry is a `\b`-anchored exact-token regex, so a misspelled keyword simply fails to match and falls through to `none` — the safe direction, never a false affirmative match. | Low | N/A | **Confirmed safe, no defect** |
| 5 | **Adversarial wording** ("ignore what I said before, disconnect google calendar") — considered and judged not to reveal an additional actionable defect beyond fixes #1/#2: this parser only ever processes the user's own voice/typed input (never third-party or model-generated text), so there is no trust boundary for adversarial phrasing to cross the way there would be for an LLM processing untrusted external text — a genuinely-intended instruction phrased unusually is still a genuine instruction, and `confirm_required`'s real dialog already gates the one case (an immediate `safe_action`) where phrasing alone could cause unwanted harm, which fixes #1/#2 address for the specific patterns (questions, negation) that actually change intent. | N/A | N/A | **Considered, not a distinct defect from #1/#2** |
| 6 | **Explanatory language executing** (the brief's own named requirement) — fixes #1 and #2 together are the direct answer: a question about a command and a negated command are the two concrete shapes "explanatory language" takes in this registry, and both are now excluded from `CONFIRM_ACTIONS`/`SAFE_ACTIONS` matching. | N/A | N/A | **Addressed by fixes #1/#2** |
| 7 | **Sensitive mutations still require proposal/approval** — re-confirmed unchanged: `confirm_required` never executes anything itself (only `App.tsx`'s `ConfirmDialog` Confirm button does, on explicit user click), and Calendar/memory/record-proposing phrases remain deliberately unmatched by every table in this registry, falling through to the existing Phase 8 propose→approve→execute lifecycle exactly as before (`registry.test.ts`'s pre-existing proposal-lifecycle-never-bypassed test, unchanged and still passing). | N/A | N/A | **Confirmed safe, no defect** |

### Fixes

Two new module-level patterns in `frontend/src/commands/registry.ts`: `NEGATION_PATTERN` (`don't`/`do not`/`never`/`shouldn't`/`should not`/`won't`/`will not`/`not now`) and `QUESTION_PATTERN` (a leading `what`/`how`/`why`/`when`/`who`/`which`/`can you`/`could you`/`would you`/`does`/`is`/`are`/`will`/`should`, or — checked separately against the un-normalized text, since `normalize()` strips trailing punctuation before `QUESTION_PATTERN` would ever see it — a trailing `?`). A single `explanatoryOnly` boolean (either pattern matching) gates both the `CONFIRM_ACTIONS` and `SAFE_ACTIONS` loops in `parseCommand` — skipped entirely when true, falling through to `SENSITIVE_CONTROLS` (a `focus_control` — deliberately *not* gated, since it only ever navigates and highlights, never activates anything, so a negated or question-phrased mention is harmless either way) or ultimately `blocked`/`none`. Both patterns were deliberately scoped conservatively (e.g. `stop`/`cancel`/`avoid` were considered for `NEGATION_PATTERN` and excluded as too likely to false-positive on ordinary affirmative sentences containing those words for unrelated reasons — over-triggering here only ever makes the system *more* conservative, never less safe, but unnecessary refusals are still a real UX cost worth minimizing).

19 new test cases across two new `describe` blocks in `registry.test.ts` (69 → 88): 9 for negation (each of the 4 `CONFIRM_ACTIONS`/`SAFE_ACTIONS` entries with multiple negation phrasings, plus a control case confirming un-negated commands are unaffected), 10 for questions (multiple question-word and trailing-`?` phrasings across both tables, plus a control case). Both fixes were verified by the same revert-and-confirm-red discipline as D83: the `if (!explanatoryOnly)` guards were temporarily replaced with `if (true)`, confirmed 8 of the 9 negation tests then failed (proving they weren't vacuous), and restored to exactly their fixed state (diffed byte-for-byte against a backup to confirm).

### Verification

Full frontend suite: **185/185 pass** (166 before this audit, +19 new). `tsc -b` and `vite build` both pass. No backend file touched by this half of the audit.

### Remaining Mac-only checks

None specific to this entry — this is pure deterministic TypeScript logic, fully verified by unit tests; no voice hardware, Keychain, or OAuth involvement. The general Phase 6 real-Mac acceptance items (a real push-to-talk voice question, real TTS) remain exactly as outstanding as recorded under D72/D77/D78/D79/D80, unaffected by this pass.

### Deliberately deferred

Extending `NEGATION_PATTERN`/`QUESTION_PATTERN` to also gate `SENSITIVE_CONTROLS`'s `focus_control` matches was considered and deliberately not done — that result kind never activates anything (navigates and highlights only, explicitly labeled "shows, does not activate" in the UI), so a negated or question-phrased mention producing a `focus_control` is harmless, and adding the guard there would only be defensive-for-its-own-sake rather than closing a real gap.

**Boundary respected**: no frontend visual/design change, no new dependency, no backend file touched, no Git operation, no product feature added — this is a bug-fix-and-test pass on existing deterministic command-parsing logic only.

**Why**: the two fixed patterns are the concrete mechanism behind the audit brief's own framing — "confirm that explanatory language never executes" is not an abstract goal here, it's exactly "a question and a negation must not resolve to the same result as the command they're talking about," which is precisely what `explanatoryOnly` now guarantees for the two result kinds (`safe_action`, `confirm_required`) capable of doing anything real.
**How to apply**: any future addition to `CONFIRM_ACTIONS` or `SAFE_ACTIONS` automatically inherits this protection, since both loops are already gated by the shared `explanatoryOnly` check — no per-entry opt-in needed. A new result kind that can execute something real (not just navigate/highlight) should be added to the `explanatoryOnly` gate's scope, following the same reasoning recorded here for why `SENSITIVE_CONTROLS` is deliberately excluded.

## D85 — Bounded long-horizon durability pass: realistic multi-year scale (52,939 rows, 5 years) and orphaned export-temp-file cleanup

**Context**: a final, narrowly-bounded follow-up to D83/D84's reliability audit, covering exactly two remaining gaps Bernardo named: (1) a materially larger, multi-year scale test (the D83 benchmark covered one year, ~2,500 rows) spanning every table a long-lived installation would actually accumulate data in, and (2) a decision on whether Jarvis's export mechanism can leak orphaned temporary files after a process interruption, with narrowly-scoped, tested cleanup if the risk is real. Explicitly not another broad audit — no new categories of finding were sought outside these two.

### 1. Realistic multi-year scale (`tests/test_scale_benchmarks_multiyear.py`)

**Dataset**: 5 years (1,825 days) of fictional, deliberately heavy single-user use, generated via efficient bulk inserts (not the real service layer — that write path was already validated at moderate scale by D83's one-year benchmark; this file's purpose is read/query performance at volume) across every table the brief named:

| Table | Rows |
|---|---|
| conversations | 1,560 |
| messages | 18,720 |
| agent_runs | 1,560 |
| context_snapshots | 1,560 |
| memory_items | 2,900 |
| memory_versions | 2,900 |
| structured_records | 3,900 |
| google_health_daily_summaries | 1,825 |
| google_health_sessions | 2,738 |
| calendar_events | 6,000 |
| documents | 80 |
| document_chunks (+ document_fts) | 800 |
| action_proposals | 1,400 |
| action_audit_events | 2,800 |
| routine_runs | 3,910 |
| integration_sync_runs | 100 (intentionally capped — the real app's own `_trim_history` never keeps more than 50/provider; generating more would be unrealistic, not just unnecessary) |
| domain_summary_versions | 150 |
| **Total** | **52,939** — within the requested 50,000-100,000 range |

**A schema subtlety found and worked around while writing the generator, not a defect**: `MemoryItem.current_version_id` / `MemoryVersion.memory_item_id`, `DomainSummary.current_version_id` / `DomainSummaryVersion.domain_summary_id`, and `Skill.current_version_id` / `SkillVersion.skill_id` are each a genuine pair of mutually-referencing foreign keys — by design, since a memory/summary/skill's "current version" and its version history are meant to point at each other. Bulk-inserting either side first violates the other's FK. This is not a defect (the real service functions — `memory_service.create_memory`, `domain_summary_service.set_domain_summary`, the skill-creation path — already sequence this correctly as two real statements, an insert then an update, for exactly this reason); the generator was written to insert with `current_version_id=NULL` first, then the version rows, then a bulk `UPDATE`, matching that same sequencing at volume.

**Thresholds — defined before the numbers were seen**, as generous personal-app upper bounds (an order of magnitude past what a MacBook Pro M2 Pro should ever need for one user's own SQLite database): cold startup 3s, FTS search 1.5s, context construction 2s, conversation list 1.5s, message load 1s, integration/routine history reads 1s each, export 60s, validate 30s, restore 90s, database size 500MB.

**Measurements** (all far inside every threshold):

| Operation | Result | Threshold |
|---|---|---|
| Dataset generation | 1.17s | 180s |
| Database size | 18.93 MB | 500 MB |
| Cold startup (engine + first query) | 1.1 ms | 3s |
| FTS search (2,900+ memories) | 1.6 ms | 1.5s |
| Context construction (5 years of BODY history) | 11.2 ms | 2s |
| Conversation list (260 conversations) | 1.0 ms | 1.5s |
| Message load | 0.3 ms | 1s |
| Integration history read (sync history + 6,000 calendar events + 1,825 health summaries) | 59.1 ms | 1s |
| Routine history read | 1.7 ms | 1s |
| Export | 0.46s | 60s |
| Archive size (compressed) | 4.91 MB | — |
| Validate | 0.16s | 30s |
| Restore | 0.44s | 90s |
| Post-restore write | 4.7 ms | — |

**Disposition: no code change.** Per the explicit instruction not to optimize a number merely because it looks imperfect, and since every measurement is one to four orders of magnitude inside its threshold, this benchmark's finding is confirmation, not a defect: Jarvis's SQLite-based, single-user architecture has enormous headroom at genuinely realistic long-horizon personal-scale volumes. Restored-database writability was also confirmed with a genuine post-restore write (a new conversation, committed, then read back) against the restored database's own fresh engine — not merely a read-only check.

### 2. Orphaned export temporary files (`app/export_service.py`, `app/main.py`)

**Inspection**: `create_export()` writes two scratch files directly into `exports_dir` before the finished archive is atomically renamed into place — `.{filename}.tmp-{pid}-{millis}` (the in-progress ZIP) and `.{filename}.dbsnapshot-{pid}` (a raw SQLite backup-API snapshot, normally deleted in a `finally` block). A process killed between either file being created and the point that would consume or delete it — the `finally` block for the dbsnapshot file, or the `os.replace()` for the .tmp file — skips that cleanup entirely, with no other code path anywhere ever revisiting either file. **The risk is real**: repeated interruptions (a laptop losing power, a `kill -9`, an OOM kill) during export could accumulate an unbounded number of these files indefinitely, silently consuming disk space. This is disk hygiene, never a correctness or security issue — nothing ever reads these files back, and a real completed export is never named this way.

**Fix**: `export_service.cleanup_stale_export_temp_files(settings, max_age_seconds=3600)` (new function), called once at every backend startup (`app/main.py`'s lifespan, alongside the existing due-backup-check and interrupted-execution-recovery calls, wrapped in the same try/except-and-log pattern so a cleanup failure can never block startup). Safety, exactly per the stated constraints:
* **Only Jarvis's own files**: a precise regex (`^\.jarvis-export-\d{8}-\d{6}\.zip\.(?:tmp|dbsnapshot)-\d+(?:-\d+)?$`) matching exactly what `create_export()` itself generates — not a broad `.tmp-*` glob — so a real completed export (never dot-prefixed) or any unrelated file a user placed in `exports_dir` is structurally unmatchable.
* **Only inside `exports_dir`, never recursive**: `Path.iterdir()` only, never `os.walk`/`glob`/`rglob`; never touches system `/tmp` (the separate Hermes-profile-export temp directory already uses Python's own `TemporaryDirectory` context manager and was deliberately left alone — out of this fix's stated scope).
* **No broad globs, no unresolved paths**: every candidate's resolved path (`Path.resolve()`) must still have `exports_dir`'s own resolved path as its parent before it is ever unlinked — defeats a symlink placed inside `exports_dir` pointing elsewhere, even though the name-pattern check alone already makes such a symlink's existence far-fetched.
* **Completed exports never touched**: guaranteed structurally by the dot-prefix requirement in the pattern above.
* **An age threshold protects an active export**: judged by the file's own `st_mtime`; anything younger than `max_age_seconds` (default 3,600s — this container's own export benchmark above completed in under half a second even at 5-years scale, so this threshold exists purely to guarantee a genuinely slow run, e.g. a large Hermes profile export, is never mistaken for stale) is always left alone.
* **Cleanup failure never blocks startup**: wrapped in try/except in `main.py`, logged and continued, matching the existing pattern for `run_due_backups` and `expire_interrupted_executions`.

**Tests** (`tests/test_export.py::TestCleanupStaleExportTempFiles`, 9 new cases): a valid stale `.tmp` file removed; a valid stale `.dbsnapshot` file removed; a recent file matching the pattern never removed; four kinds of unrelated file (including near-misses on the pattern) never removed; a genuinely completed export never removed regardless of age; a symlink inside `exports_dir` pointing outside it never followed or removed (its target file's content and existence verified untouched); a mixed-contents sweep removing only the two matching stale files and leaving a real export alone; a missing `exports_dir` handled as a safe no-op; and a full end-to-end test using a real second `app.main` lifespan startup (`restart_client_factory`, the same pattern D83 used for `expire_interrupted_executions`) proving the wiring, not just the function in isolation. Every safety property was verified by the same revert-and-confirm-red discipline as D83/D84 — the parent-directory (symlink) check, the dot-prefix (completed-export) requirement, and the age check were each independently disabled in turn and confirmed to make the corresponding test fail before being restored to their fixed state (diffed byte-for-byte against a backup). One test (`test_a_symlink_escaping_exports_dir_is_never_followed_or_removed`) initially passed vacuously — for being "too young" rather than for correctly detecting the symlink escape — until the target file's mtime was explicitly aged past the threshold to isolate the parent-directory check as the only remaining reason for it to pass; this is recorded because it is the same class of mistake D83 caught in the `busy_timeout` test, now caught a second time in a different file.

### Verification

Full backend suite: **467/467 pass** (451 after D83/D84, +16 from this pass: 9 export-cleanup tests, 7 multi-year scale tests). No frontend file was touched by this pass, so the frontend suite was not re-run, per the explicit instruction to do so only if frontend code changes. The shared fixture-backed preview (fictional data, ports 8000/4173) from the earlier Phase 6 operational-redesign pass was confirmed still running and untouched throughout.

**Boundary respected**: isolated fictional data only; no real credentials, Keychain, Google, Hermes, model, microphone, or personal data; no frontend change; no migration (no genuine schema defect was found — the mutual-FK ordering above is an existing, correct design, not a bug); no Git operation. Phase 6 and Phase 11 remain exactly as open as before — this pass closes neither.

**Why**: a durability pass earns its name by measuring against thresholds decided in advance, not by hunting for a number to shave — the multi-year benchmark's actual value here is the confirmation that nothing degrades at realistic long-horizon scale, recorded honestly rather than manufacturing a change to justify the pass. The export-cleanup fix follows the exact same safety template D83's `expire_interrupted_executions` and D84's command-safety guards already established: identify a real but narrow gap, fix it with the smallest correct change, and prove every safety property with a test that has itself been shown capable of failing.
**How to apply**: any future bulk-data-generation script for testing should watch for mutually-referencing FK pairs (search for a model whose own primary key is also referenced by a "current version" style column) and insert-then-update rather than assume a single insert order will satisfy both sides. Any future startup-time cleanup sweep should follow `cleanup_stale_export_temp_files`'s pattern exactly: a precise pattern (never a broad glob), a single-directory `iterdir()` (never recursive), a resolved-parent check (defeats symlink escapes), and an age threshold — and its regression tests should each be verified capable of failing by temporarily disabling the specific safety property they claim to guard, the same way this entry's symlink test had to be corrected mid-review.

## D86 — Phase 12A: a shared, deterministic situational-briefing assembler, and a concise Home "mission strip" (NOW/NEXT/WATCH)

**Context**: Bernardo asked for a new bounded phase — when Jarvis opens, Home should answer "what needs my attention now / what's coming next / what's stale or failing" in 3-5 concise, source-backed items, closer to an executive briefing than a dashboard. The explicit instruction was to reuse the existing Phase 10B Morning Briefing machinery wherever appropriate rather than building a second, contradictory briefing engine, to keep everything deterministic (no model call to generate/rank/summarize), to respect the already-recorded BODY-in/MIND-and-PEOPLE-out privacy selection, and not to enable any routine, Hermes toolset, or touch OAuth/credentials/schedules/real data.

**Architecture — one shared assembler, two consumers**: `backend/app/briefing_service.py` is new and is the single place both consumers get their underlying facts from:
1. **Phase 10B's Morning Briefing routine** (`app/routine_service.py`) — its five private section-builders (`_life_tasks_section`, `_calendar_section`, `_path_deadlines_section`, `_build_checkpoints_section`, `_body_section`) were refactored to call four new shared, public functions in `briefing_service.py` (`open_records`, `todays_calendar_events`, `latest_google_health_summary`, `domain_id_by_slug`) instead of re-querying the same tables independently. The refactor was verified byte-for-byte behavior-preserving: the full pre-existing `test_routine_service.py` suite (21 tests, asserting exact section titles/line text/source-refs) passes unchanged, and the full backend suite (467→494, +27) passes with zero regressions.
2. **The new on-demand Home briefing** (`GET /api/briefing/home`) — `assemble_home_briefing()` calls the same shared fact-gathering functions plus new candidate-generation logic (calendar imminence, overdue/due-soon LIFE/PATH records, pending/failed action proposals, failed/stale integration syncs, failed routine runs, a recent BUILD checkpoint, and BODY staleness when opted in), producing a flat list of typed `BriefingItem`s that is deduplicated, sorted, and capped at 5.

This is the "one shared assembler" the brief asked for: the Morning Briefing routine and the Home briefing can never independently re-derive (and silently disagree about) what "today's Calendar events" or "open LIFE tasks" actually are — there is exactly one query for each of those facts in the codebase now, not two.

**Priority and privacy rules, exactly as implemented** (`briefing_service.py`, fully documented in its own module docstring and inline comments, not just here):
* Three categories, ranked in this fixed order: `now` (immediate/overdue) → `next` (upcoming today/soon) → `watch` (failure/stale/pending/notable). A category with nothing in it is simply absent — never a forced empty heading.
* Within a category, items sort by an explicit integer `priority` (lower first), then a deterministic tie-break (`domain_slug`, `source_type`, `source_ids`) — never insertion order or anything unstable. A documented, deliberate judgment call: an imminent Calendar event (starting within 30 minutes) always outranks an overdue task within NOW, since a meeting is more acutely time-critical than a task that has already been overdue for days — this is Jarvis's own tie-break, not something the brief mandated precisely, and is called out as such in both the code comment and a dedicated regression test.
* A `life_task`/`path_deadline`'s `due_date` is a free-form, unvalidated string (`app/structured_records.py` never enforced a format) — a value that doesn't parse as ISO-8601 is simply not date-triaged for Home rather than crashing or being guessed at.
* Deduplication: a `(category, source_type, source_ids)` key collapses any accidental duplicate before capping.
* Cap: `HOME_BRIEFING_MAX_ITEMS = 5` — NOW items are never starved by lower-priority NEXT/WATCH items since categories are ranked first.
* **Privacy boundary, structural not just a flag**: a new single-row `briefing_settings` table (migration `0012`, seeded `include_body=1, include_mind=0, include_people=0` — Bernardo's already-recorded Phase 10B selection) gates BODY (Google Health). `include_mind`/`include_people` exist on the settings row and the assembler's signature for shape-parity with the routine's own per-domain opt-in, but **gate nothing** — there is no code path from MIND/PEOPLE tables into `assemble_home_briefing` at all, regardless of their value. This is deliberately stronger than a runtime check: a test creates real `mind_checkin`/`people_interaction` records, calls the assembler with both flags forced `True`, and asserts neither ever appears. `PUT /api/briefing/settings` only accepts `include_body`; MIND/PEOPLE always persist `False` no matter what's sent, enforced server-side (`set_include_body`), not merely by the frontend never offering the control.
* **No source is ever silently omitted vs. genuinely empty**: `HomeBriefing.sources` reports `ok`/`stale`/`unavailable`/`not_connected` per relevant provider (Calendar, Google Health) so the frontend can say "some sources are unavailable" truthfully rather than a stale integration reading as "nothing happened."
* **Provenance on every item**: stable `id`, `source_type`, `source_ids`, a human `reason`, `source_timestamp`, a `freshness` label (`current`/`cached`/`stale`/`unavailable`), and a `classification` (`factual` — nothing in this phase ever sets `inferred`; no health score, medical interpretation, or invented insight is computed anywhere in this module).
* **No model call anywhere**: verified two ways — an AST-based unit test asserts the module imports no provider module and calls no `send_turn`-named function, and an HTTP-level test confirms `GET /api/briefing/home` never changes `/api/agent/status`'s reported state. The only model-reaching action anywhere near this feature is "Discuss with Jarvis," which — exactly like Phase 10B's routine pattern — reuses the existing `createGeneralConversation`/`sendTurn` functions against the existing, already-model-using turn endpoint; no new backend route can ever initiate a model call.

**API** (`backend/app/routers/briefing.py`): `GET /api/briefing/home`, `GET`/`PUT /api/briefing/settings`. All three are plain reads/local-config writes, matching the existing precedent that configuring Jarvis's own local preferences is not a Phase 8 action proposal (it has no external side effect of its own — same reasoning as the Phase 10B/10A schedule-config endpoints).

**Frontend — a "mission strip," not another rounded card** (`frontend/src/components/BriefingStrip.tsx`, `frontend/src/views/Home.tsx`): a slim, dense panel sits below the orbit on Home, in the same near-black/matte/thin-violet-border language the console interior already uses (D75), kept visually quiet enough that it never competes with the core/orbit for attention — reusing existing design tokens throughout (`--status-warn`/`--status-error`/`--accent-cyan` for tone, never a new color). Every item shows an explicit NOW/NEXT/WATCH text badge (never color alone) plus a left-border tone accent (amber=attention, red=failure, cyan=neutral), matching the existing `.queue-item` left-accent convention (D80) rather than inventing a new one. Three explicit actions: **Refresh** (calls `GET /api/briefing/home` again — deterministic, no cache invalidation logic needed since nothing is cached client-side), **Discuss with Jarvis** (disabled when there's nothing to discuss; opens a fresh general conversation titled "Situational briefing discussion" and sends the rendered items as one turn, with the reply visibly labeled "Jarvis (model response)" exactly like Routine Centre's existing convention), and **Read briefing aloud** (reuses the existing `synthesizeSpeech`/Edge TTS path from Phase 5 directly — a one-off playback, deliberately not wired into `useVoiceCapture`'s full state machine, since this is passive listening, not a conversational turn; never plays automatically on page load). Clicking an item calls `onNavigate` — the exact same `navigateToTarget` function `App.tsx` already exposes to the command-palette/voice command layer — after validating the backend-supplied `link_target` string against a new `isNavigateTarget()` runtime guard added to `commands/registry.ts` (reusing its existing `CENTRE_ALIASES`/`DOMAIN_ALIASES` tables as the source of truth); an unrecognized or absent `link_target` never navigates anywhere and the button is simply disabled. The empty state renders literally "No immediate items." — no manufactured advice — and a source-unavailable note appears only when `HomeBriefing.sources` genuinely reports one.

**A real bug found and fixed during this pass**: a naive `briefing?.sources.filter(...)` optional-chain (guarding only the `.sources` access, not the subsequent `.filter` call) crashed whenever `briefing` was `null` — caught not by a unit test but by the full frontend suite, when a stray process already listening on `127.0.0.1:8000` in this container (an artifact of the sandbox, not a real Jarvis backend) let an unmocked `fetchHomeBriefing()` call in pre-existing App-level tests actually resolve with `{}` instead of rejecting as intended. Fixed by deriving a local `items`/`unavailableSources` with full `?? []` fallbacks, and — separately, for test determinism — every App/Diagnostic-level test file that renders `Home` (directly or via `App`) now explicitly mocks `fetchHomeBriefing`, matching the existing convention that every other Home-level network call is always mocked in those suites.

**A second, unrelated lint fix**: the first draft of "Read briefing aloud" stored its `<audio>` element in a `useRef` that was also read inside an unmount-cleanup `useEffect`, which `oxlint`'s `react(immutability)` rule flagged as a new warning category (D80's "no new warning categories" bar). Root-caused to the ref's `.current` being read inside any effect at all, regardless of how the mutation site was restructured (moving the assignment earlier, before an `await`, changed nothing) — resolved by holding the audio element in `useState` instead of a ref, with its own small `useEffect(() => () => readAudioEl?.pause(), [readAudioEl])` cleanup; confirmed back to the pre-existing zero-`immutability`/stable-`set-state-in-effect`-count baseline.

**Container browser QA** (a temporary Playwright/Chromium harness, `/tmp`, never added to `frontend/package.json`/lockfiles — same established pattern as D72/D75/D80): the real production build, `page.route`-intercepted with fictional fixture data (no real backend), checked at 1440×900, 1280×800, 1024×768, and 820×900. Zero horizontal overflow at any viewport; zero console/page errors; the orbit/core and the briefing strip confirmed both visible with no vertical overlap at every viewport (the strip never obstructs the orbit); a real `getComputedStyle(...).transform` read on a ring layer confirmed `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` still holds `"none"` (unaffected by this pass, verified again anyway); a real click-through on "Renew passport" navigated to the LIFE domain view; keyboard Tab moved focus from Refresh → Read aloud → Discuss with Jarvis → each briefing item in order; "Discuss with Jarvis" produced a visibly labeled fixture model reply. A full axe-core (`wcag2a`+`wcag2aa`) sweep at 1440×900 found **zero violations**. The empty-state screenshot was inspected directly and shows literally "No immediate items." with Read-aloud/Discuss both correctly disabled — not a fabricated "everything's fine" message.

**Boundary respected**: no routine was enabled (all three `routine_schedules` rows remain seeded `enabled=0`, untouched by this pass); Hermes toolsets were never touched (no Hermes call exists anywhere in the new code, confirmed by the AST test above); no OAuth/credential/real-account/real-`~/JarvisData` state was touched — every backend test uses the existing isolated `data_dir`/`db_session` fixtures, and every frontend/QA check uses fictional fixture data; no sub-agent, Hermes skill, hook, memory, or cron was introduced; no Git operation was performed.

**Why**: reusing the Phase 10B fact-gathering functions rather than re-implementing "today's Calendar events" a second time is what actually prevents the two features from silently drifting apart over time — the explicit design goal stated in the brief. Keeping the assembler 100% deterministic and provenance-carrying (rather than letting a model rank/summarize) is what makes "Context used"-style auditability (CLAUDE.md §9's "see what context and tools affected an answer") apply to Home's first screen too, not just conversation turns.

**How to apply**: any future new "what does Jarvis think is important" surface should call into `briefing_service.py`'s shared fact-gathering functions rather than re-querying `CalendarEventCache`/`StructuredRecord`/`GoogleHealthDailySummary` directly — that's the whole point of having extracted them. Any future sensitive-domain source added to the Home briefing must follow BODY's exact pattern: an explicit, persisted, restart-surviving settings flag that is checked at the one single call site that reads that domain's data, never a default-on flag and never a flag that's accepted by the update endpoint without being enforced server-side.

**Verification**: backend 494/494 (467 + 27 new: `test_briefing_service.py` 21, `test_briefing_api.py` 5, `test_migration_0012.py` 1 — zero real Google/Keychain/Hermes/model contact). Frontend 192/192 (185 + 7 new, `Home.briefing.test.tsx`), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories, confirmed by an explicit before/after category count) all pass.

## D87 — Phase 12B: briefing continuity — stable identity vs. content fingerprint, new/changed/ongoing/resolved/reopened classification, false-resolution protection, and acknowledge/snooze as a pure presentation preference

**Context**: Bernardo asked Home's briefing to stop reading like a stateless dashboard that repeats the same information every fetch — it should know what's new, what changed, what's ongoing, what resolved, and let him acknowledge or temporarily snooze an item without ever touching the underlying data, while staying just as short and just as deterministic as Phase 12A (D86).

**Architecture — extends the one shared assembler, does not fork it**: everything lives in `app/briefing_service.py` (now also home to Phase 12B's continuity logic) and four new tables (migration `0013`, forward-only, `0001`-`0012` untouched):
* `briefing_snapshots` — a bounded, deduped audit trail of candidate-set generations, tagged with a `consumer` (`home` | `morning_briefing` — the actual comparison-baseline lineage) and a finer `trigger` label (`home_view`/`home_refresh`/`morning_briefing_manual`/`morning_briefing_scheduled`/`morning_briefing_startup_catchup`, audit-only, never affecting comparison logic).
* `briefing_item_states` — the Home-only per-identity continuity ledger (`stable_key` unique, `status` active/resolved, `current_fingerprint`, cached `last_title`/`last_subtitle`/`last_category`/`last_link_target` for a truthful resolved-display, `reopened_count`). The Morning Briefing routine never reads or writes this table at all — its own snapshot row (see below) is the *entire* extent of its Phase 12B footprint, which is what structurally guarantees "a background Morning Briefing run must not silently consume Home's baseline" rather than merely documenting the intent.
* `briefing_acknowledgements` / `briefing_snoozes` — local presentation-preference tables, each row keyed to an exact `(stable_key, fingerprint)` pair with a frozen display-text snapshot (`title_snapshot` etc.) taken at acknowledge/snooze time, so the "Acknowledged & snoozed" history view never needs to re-derive from a candidate that may since have resolved away.

**Stable identity vs. content fingerprint — the two-identifier model, exactly as specified**: every `BriefingItem` now carries both `id` (the stable identity — unchanged across time for the same underlying concern) and a new `fingerprint` (a 16-hex-char SHA-256 digest over a small, explicit, per-source-documented set of already-normalized fields — never a raw provider payload, secret, or token). Getting the *identity* boundary right required correcting three of Phase 12A's original id formats, since they had accidentally folded volatile state into the identity itself:
* `integration_sync:{provider}:error` / `:stale` → `integration_sync:{provider}` — a provider's sync health is one continuous concern; the error-vs-stale distinction and the exact error text now live in the fingerprint, so a warning quietly becoming a hard failure reads as `changed`, not as one identity resolving while an unrelated new one appears.
* `google_health:no_data` / `google_health:{summary.id}` → the fixed slot `google_health:freshness` — there is only ever one "is BODY data fresh enough" concern, not one per summary row.
* `routine_run:{run.id}` → `routine_run:{routine_type}` — the concern is "does this routine currently run cleanly"; a *new* failing run (a different `RoutineRun.id`) is exactly what the fingerprint captures as `changed`, correctly re-flagging a fresh failure rather than either silently reusing a stale identity or spawning a new one per run.

Per-source fingerprint fields (documented inline at each construction site in `briefing_service.py`, not just here): calendar events use `{title, start, end, all_day, category}`; LIFE/PATH records use `{title, due_date, category}`; both deliberately include `category` (NOW/NEXT), so a NEXT→NOW transition ("became imminent"/"became overdue") is itself a `changed` classification — the explicit, deliberate implementation of the brief's "an item becoming more urgent counts as changed." Pending/failed action-proposal aggregates fingerprint `{count, sorted(ids)}`, so the count staying identical while the underlying set actually changed still classifies correctly. Integration-sync and BODY-freshness fingerprints deliberately *exclude* the exact timestamp/day-count (`last_sync_at`, `days_stale`) — including either would make the item read as "changed" on every single tick it stays broken/stale, which is exactly the noise Phase 12B was asked to eliminate, not reintroduce.

**Classification rules** (`_reconcile_ledger`, computed over the complete, un-capped candidate set — never the visible top-5, so priority-cap churn can never manufacture a false `new` or `resolved`, verified by a dedicated test seeding 7 tied-priority overdue tasks and confirming all 7 ledger rows stay `active` across repeated passes even though only 5 are ever visible):
* no ledger row for this identity → `new`.
* ledger row exists, `status == 'resolved'` → `reopened` (and `reopened_count` increments — kept as an explicit, auditable counter, not inferred after the fact).
* ledger row exists, `status == 'active'`, fingerprint unchanged → `ongoing`.
* ledger row exists, `status == 'active'`, fingerprint changed → `changed`.
* an `active` ledger row whose identity is absent from this pass's candidates is marked `resolved` **only if** that identity's `source_type` evaluated successfully this pass (see false-resolution protection below) — otherwise left exactly as it was.

A freshly-resolved identity gets exactly one synthesized `resolved`-state display item (title from the ledger's last-known-truthful `last_title`, reason "This condition no longer applies.") — self-limiting to the single pass where the transition actually happened, which is what makes "resolved items are shown sparingly" fall out of the state machine itself rather than needing a separate display-side suppression rule.

**False-resolution protection**: `_gather_candidates` wraps every source's query in its own try/except, producing a `SourceEvaluation(source_type, ok, detail)` per source regardless of whether any other source failed — one source's exception can never crash the whole assembly and, critically, can never make *that source's* previously-active identities look resolved. Verified live (not just asserted) by monkeypatching `open_records` to raise mid-pass: the affected item vanishes from that pass's visible output (there is genuinely no fresh data to show), the ledger row stays `active` untouched, and the very next successful pass correctly reports `ongoing` — never `reopened`, since it was never actually marked resolved. `include_body=False` and "this domain doesn't exist yet" are both modeled as a *successful* no-op evaluation, never conflated with a failure.

**Acknowledge/snooze — presentation only, enforced structurally, not by convention**: `acknowledge_item`/`snooze_item` always resolve the item's *current* fingerprint from the ledger server-side (`_current_ledger_row`) — a client can never submit a stale or fabricated fingerprint to acknowledge/snooze the wrong version of an item. Suppression (`is_suppressed`) is a lookup on the exact `(stable_key, fingerprint, status='active')` triple, which is what makes "must immediately break if the item materially changes" fall out structurally: a changed fingerprint simply no longer matches any stored acknowledgement/snooze row, with no explicit "invalidate" step required anywhere. Snoozing accepts only the fixed `1h`/`4h`/`tomorrow_morning`/`1w` enum (validated both by Pydantic's `Literal` at the API boundary and again by the service function itself) — never an arbitrary client timestamp. `tomorrow_morning` is computed DST-safely (`_tomorrow_morning_utc`, constructing the candidate directly in the target IANA timezone's wall-clock terms before converting to UTC, mirroring `routine_service._next_occurrence_utc` exactly), verified against a real Europe/Lisbon spring-forward transition. `expire_due_snoozes` runs at the start of every `assemble_home_briefing` call, so an expired snooze's item reliably reappears on the very next fetch with no separate cron/poll needed. Neither function ever creates a Phase 8 `ActionProposal` or touches any capability — confirmed by a dedicated test asserting the Actions Centre listing stays empty throughout an acknowledge→restore cycle.

**Snapshot spam prevention**: `record_snapshot` computes a content digest over the full candidate set's `(stable_key, fingerprint)` pairs; a repeat within `SNAPSHOT_DEDUPE_WINDOW_SECONDS` (60s) with an identical digest reuses the existing row rather than inserting a near-duplicate — verified with 5 rapid consecutive HTTP `GET /api/briefing/home` calls collapsing into exactly one snapshot row and one shared `change_state` across every call after the first, which is the practical, testable proxy for "concurrent refreshes cannot create contradictory baselines" (this project's existing `busy_timeout=5000` SQLite setting, D83, is what actually serializes any genuinely concurrent write at the database layer). Bounded retention mirrors `routine_service`'s established pattern exactly: `SNAPSHOT_RETENTION_PER_CONSUMER` (200) trims on insert; a new startup sweep, `cleanup_old_briefing_state` (wired into `main.py`'s lifespan alongside the existing due-backup/interrupted-execution/stale-export-temp-file sweeps, same try/except-and-log-never-block-startup discipline), prunes `resolved` ledger rows and `restored`/`expired` acknowledge/snooze rows past a 30-day retention window — an `active` row of any kind is never touched. This matters concretely for a fast-churning source like Calendar: a distinct `calendar_event` identity is created every single day by construction, so without this sweep the ledger would grow without bound.

**Morning Briefing stays untouched in substance**: the routine's own `OutputSection`/line rendering, domain-selection logic, and single-flight/backoff scheduling are exactly as Phase 10B left them. The only change is one new call, `record_snapshot(consumer="morning_briefing", trigger=f"morning_briefing_{trigger}", ...)`, added to `run_routine` right after a successful run, using a lightweight digest derived straight from the routine's own rendered line text (no per-item fingerprinting, since nothing reads it back for change-state display) — proving, by construction rather than assertion, that a scheduled/manual/startup-catchup routine run can never touch `BriefingItemState` or any acknowledge/snooze table.

**API** (`app/routers/briefing.py`, extending the existing route family rather than a parallel one): `GET /api/briefing/home?trigger=home_view|home_refresh` (both hit the same `home` comparison baseline — refreshing can never itself manufacture a spurious change, verified directly), `GET /api/briefing/history?consumer=&limit=`, and `POST /api/briefing/items/{stable_key}/acknowledge|snooze|restore`. `{stable_key}` is always one of the safe, pre-existing identity strings (never a raw title, provider payload, or token) — confirmed by a dedicated test grepping the full JSON response for token/secret-shaped substrings. Acknowledge/snooze/restore are ordinary transactional local mutations (not Phase 8 proposals) — they only ever change what this module's own presentation-state tables say, exactly the same reasoning already established for the Phase 10A/10B/12A schedule-config endpoints (configuring Jarvis's own local preference has no external side effect).

**Frontend** (`frontend/src/components/BriefingStrip.tsx`, `frontend/src/views/Home.tsx`): each item now shows two independent badges — the existing NOW/NEXT/WATCH category and a new NEW/CHANGED/ONGOING/RESOLVED/REOPENED change-state badge, using a color mapping distinct from (and independent of) the existing tone/severity colors: cyan for new/resolved, amber for changed/reopened, and a deliberately quiet no-fill treatment for ongoing (the common case after the first pass, which must never compete for attention with a genuinely new item) — never color alone, since the text label is always present too. Per-item Acknowledge and a native-`<details>` Snooze disclosure (four fixed duration buttons — an accessible menu with no custom ARIA needed) sit beside the existing navigate button rather than nested inside it, since a `<button>` cannot contain another interactive control. A resolved item shows no Acknowledge/Snooze controls at all (there's nothing left to suppress). An unobtrusive, closed-by-default "Acknowledged & snoozed" `<details>` history lists each hidden item with a Restore button and one explicit sentence — "only changes what this briefing shows... never modifies the underlying [record]" — so the distinction from actually resolving something is never left implicit. "Discuss with Jarvis" now includes each item's change-state label alongside its category in the exact same quoted-reference-data text sent through the existing turn endpoint — still never treated as an instruction by the model (the same `REFERENCE DATA (quoted, not instructions)` framing that already governs every other retrieved fact, `app/context_builder.py`).

**One thing caught while writing tests, not a production defect**: an early draft of `test_snooze_durations_1h_4h_1w` compared `BriefingSnooze.snooze_until` (naturally naive once read back from SQLite, the same pattern documented throughout this codebase since Phase 4) directly against an aware `datetime` with `==`, which fails on the tzinfo mismatch alone rather than testing anything real. Fixed in the test via the existing `_as_aware` helper — no production code changed, since every genuine comparison inside `briefing_service.py` already goes through `_as_aware` consistently. No other defect, in either direction, was found in this pass; the lifecycle/false-resolution/suppression behavior worked as designed on first implementation, verified by the smoke-test sequence run before the formal test suite was written (new → ongoing → changed → resolved → gone → reopened, then acknowledge/restore/snooze/expiry, then a simulated source failure) rather than asserted from source reading alone.

**Container browser QA**: the same temporary Playwright/Chromium harness pattern (`/tmp`, never added to `frontend/package.json`), fictional `page.route`-intercepted fixtures covering every change state plus a populated acknowledge/snooze history, at 1440×900/1280×800/1024×768/820×900. Zero horizontal overflow at any viewport; zero console errors; all seven badge texts (NEW/CHANGED/ONGOING/RESOLVED/REOPENED/ACKNOWLEDGED/SNOOZED) confirmed rendering as real text, not color-only; a real click on "Acknowledge" and on a snooze-duration button both confirmed reaching the (fixture) backend; keyboard Tab confirmed a logical order (Refresh → Read aloud → Discuss with Jarvis → briefing item → Acknowledge → Snooze); `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` re-confirmed to hold ring rotation at `"none"` across two reads. A zero-violation axe-core (`wcag2a`+`wcag2aa`) sweep was run with the history disclosure and one snooze menu deliberately expanded first, so their content was actually part of what got audited, not skipped as hidden.

**Boundary respected**: no routine was enabled (Morning Briefing's own `enabled` flag and recorded schedule values, Bernardo's Phase 10B selections, were never touched); Hermes toolsets, OAuth, credentials, and real provider accounts were never touched; no real personal data was used anywhere (fictional fixtures and an injected clock throughout every backend and frontend test, and in the container QA pass); no sub-agent, Hermes skill, hook, memory, or cron was introduced; MIND/PEOPLE remain structurally unreadable by this feature (unchanged from D86 — re-confirmed by a Phase 12B test creating real MIND/PEOPLE records and asserting they never surface even via the continuity path); no Git operation was performed.

**Why**: the two-identifier model (stable identity vs. content fingerprint) is what lets "did the underlying concern change" be answered without either a model's judgment or a mountain of special-cased diffing logic per source — each source only has to say what identifies it and what about it actually matters, and the same small reconciliation function handles every source uniformly. Keeping acknowledge/snooze as presentation-only, enforced by never having a code path from either table into any external system, is what lets Bernardo safely dismiss noise without ever risking that dismissal being mistaken for "handled."

**How to apply**: any future new candidate source must define both an `id` that never embeds volatile state and a `fingerprint` over only the fields where a change is genuinely worth re-surfacing — copy the pattern's inline documentation at any existing `_fingerprint(...)` call site rather than inventing a new shape. Any future mutation of briefing presentation state (a third suppression mechanism, say) must key off `(stable_key, fingerprint)` exactly like acknowledge/snooze do, so a fingerprint change continues to be the one mechanism that invalidates every kind of suppression automatically.

**Verification**: backend 547/547 (494 + 53 new: `test_migration_0013.py` 1, `test_briefing_continuity.py` 23, `test_briefing_ack_snooze.py` 17, `test_briefing_api.py` +10 (5→15), `test_briefing_export_restore.py` 2 — zero real Google/Keychain/Hermes/model contact throughout). Frontend 200/200 (192 + 8 new in `Home.briefing.test.tsx`), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories, confirmed by an explicit before/after category count — identical to D86's) all pass.

## D88 — Phase 12C: Mission Focus — a small, deliberate, user-owned watchlist referencing existing sources, integrated into the shared assembler with real database-level enforcement of the five-pin limit

**Context**: Bernardo asked for a small "Mission Focus" — a handful of things he has explicitly chosen to prioritize — without turning Jarvis into an autonomous task manager or letting it decide priorities on its own. The explicit constraints were: reference real existing sources rather than copying them into a second task system, cap at five active pins enforced server-side, integrate into the Phase 12A/12B assembler rather than a parallel widget, and keep every mutation a direct user action, never a model-selected or automatic one.

**Architecture — one new table, one new service module, zero parallel systems**: `app/mission_focus_service.py` (pin/unpin/edit/reorder) and one new table (migration `0014`, forward-only, `0001`-`0013` untouched), `mission_focus_pins`. Everything that touches briefing content lives in the SAME `app/briefing_service.py` module extended in place: a new `resolve_pin_source()` function, `_merge_pin_into_item`/`_synthesize_pin_item`/`_unavailable_pin_item`/`_gather_mission_focus`/`build_mission_focus_rail`, wired into the existing `assemble_home_briefing()` pipeline between candidate-gathering and ledger reconciliation. There is no second priority system, no second fingerprint function, no second change-state machine — a pinned item is reconciled against the exact same `BriefingItemState` ledger Phase 12B already built.

**Eligible sources — narrow and typed, not arbitrary** (`MISSION_FOCUS_SOURCE_TYPES` in `app/models_mission_focus.py`): `life_task`, `path_deadline`, `build_checkpoint` (all three: existing `StructuredRecord` rows, validated by `record_type` match), `calendar_event` (`CalendarEventCache`), and `action_proposal` (`ActionProposal`). Nothing else is accepted — a `source_type` outside this fixed set is rejected by Pydantic's `Literal` before it ever reaches the service layer, and `source_id` is always resolved against the real row (never trusted to merely "look right"): a UUID belonging to the wrong `record_type`, or naming no row at all, is rejected with a clear error (`validate_and_resolve_for_pin`). **Rejected explicitly, by design**: BODY (Google Health) — no data type there is a Mission-Focus-eligible source in this phase, structurally (`assert "google_health" not in MISSION_FOCUS_SOURCE_TYPES`, a dedicated test) — and any free-form/arbitrary-JSON reference, which was never on the table at all.

**Privacy — domain resolved and enforced server-side, never client-supplied**: `MissionFocusPin.domain_slug` is always derived fresh from the real source row inside `create_pin`/`resolve_pin_source` — a pin-creation request never includes a domain field for the client to lie about. `life_task`/`path_deadline`/`build_checkpoint` are structurally locked to LIFE/PATH/BUILD by `RECORD_TYPE_DOMAIN_SLUG` (Phase 4) and `calendar_event` is always LIFE, so only `action_proposal` (whose `domain_id` can reference any of the six domains, including MIND/PEOPLE) needed an explicit check: `validate_and_resolve_for_pin` rejects any resolved `domain_slug` in `("mind", "people")` outright — verified live against a real MIND-domain `ActionProposal` and a real PEOPLE-domain one, both correctly refused, and a global (`domain_id=None`) proposal correctly accepted. A second, defense-in-depth test inserts a MIND-domain pin directly into the table (bypassing the service layer entirely) and confirms the assembler itself still never surfaces its content — the boundary holds even if the one validation call site were somehow bypassed.

**Maximum five active pins — enforced at the database level, not just in application code**: migration `0014` adds two partial unique indexes (`uq_mission_focus_active_source` on `(source_type, source_id) WHERE status='active'`, `uq_mission_focus_active_rank` on `(rank) WHERE status='active'` — a plain, non-partial unique constraint would have wrongly forbidden ever re-pinning the same source a second time after an earlier unpin, since both `'unpinned'` rows would collide) and two triggers (`trg_mission_focus_max_active_pins_insert`/`_update`) that `RAISE(ABORT, ...)` whenever an INSERT or an UPDATE would make `status='active'` while 5 active rows already exist. This is the real backstop against a race a plain "count active rows, then insert" application check cannot fully close on its own — proved directly, not just asserted, by a test that opens a second, independent raw `sqlite3` connection to the same database file (bypassing `mission_focus_service` entirely) after 5 pins are already active, and confirms the database itself still refuses a 6th. `rank`'s own CHECK constraint only enforces `>= 1` at the database level (not the full 1-5 range) — `reorder_pins` needs brief, safely-out-of-the-way temporary values (`1000+`) while permuting ranks among the same 5 rows, since SQLite has no deferrable UNIQUE constraint to lean on instead; the real 1-5 range is validated at the two service-layer call sites that ever assign a "real" rank (`create_pin`'s explicit `rank` argument, `reorder_pins`'s final assignment loop).

**A pin's whole point: staying visible beyond a source's normal ephemeral window**. Phase 12A's `_build_checkpoint_item`/`_dated_record_items` only ever surface a checkpoint within its own 2-day window or a deadline within its own 3-day due-soon window — `resolve_pin_source()` deliberately bypasses those windows entirely, resolving a pinned source directly by id regardless of age or date, which is what lets a BUILD checkpoint from 10 days ago (pinned) still appear on Home, verified by a test that confirms it's genuinely absent unpinned and genuinely present pinned.

**Merge, don't duplicate**: for the four source types whose *natural* candidate identity already matches the pin's own (`life_task:{id}`, `path_deadline:{id}`, `build_checkpoint:{id}`, `calendar_event:{id}`), `_gather_mission_focus` attaches pin metadata onto the SAME candidate object (`_merge_pin_into_item`) rather than adding a second row for the same concern — an overdue pinned task shows once, correctly still NOW-category from its own real urgency, with a `pinned`/`pin_rank` flag attached. Only when a pin's source produced no natural candidate this pass (the checkpoint-outside-its-window case above, or an `action_proposal` — which never has a natural per-proposal identity at all, only the existing aggregate `action_proposal:pending`/`:failed` counts) does `_synthesize_pin_item` create a new one. A dedicated test confirms a pinned item never appears twice.

**Fingerprint — pin metadata folded into the existing content fingerprint, not a separate mechanism**: `_merge_pin_into_item`/`_synthesize_pin_item` compute a combined fingerprint over `{natural: <the source's own already-computed fingerprint>, rank, next_action, blocker, target_at}`. This is what makes a rank, next-action, blocker, or target-date edit — or a meaningful change to the underlying source itself (title, due date, archival) — correctly classify as Phase 12B's `changed` state, verified by five separate tests each changing exactly one of those fields and confirming `changed` (and, symmetrically, a truly unchanged re-assembly confirming `ongoing`). Because acknowledge/snooze suppression already keys on the exact `(stable_key, fingerprint)` pair (D87), this single mechanism is *also* what makes "acknowledging or snoozing a pinned item never unpins it, but a meaningful pin edit still resurfaces it" fall out for free — verified directly: acknowledging a pinned item's briefing row suppresses it from the unified feed while the pin itself stays `active` and still visible in the Mission Focus rail; editing the pin's `next_action` afterward makes the old acknowledgement's fingerprint stop matching, and the item reappears.

**False-resolution protection extends to pins specifically**: each pin's `resolve_pin_source()` call is wrapped in its own try/except inside `_gather_mission_focus` — one pin's lookup failure can never crash the whole briefing, and (verified live, mirroring D87's pattern exactly) the natural, independently-gathered NOW/WATCH item for that same source is left completely alone on its own merits when the pin-specific merge step fails, with the underlying `BriefingItemState` ledger row never touched, let alone falsely marked resolved. Genuine source deletion (the row itself is gone, not merely unreachable this pass) is handled differently and deliberately: `_unavailable_pin_item` surfaces a persistent, truthful "no longer available" item (using the pin's own frozen `source_title_snapshot`, captured at pin time) for as long as the pin itself stays active — never silently dropped, since the pin still exists until Bernardo explicitly removes it, and never conflated with the one-time "resolved" confirmation a genuinely-completed source gets.

**Priority — pins boosted, never able to outrank a genuine failure or real urgency**: a new priority band, `_PRIORITY_MISSION_FOCUS_WATCH_BASE = 25`, sits between `INTEGRATION_ERROR` (20) and `INTEGRATION_STALE` (40) in Phase 12A's existing scale — a non-urgent pin (a WATCH-tier checkpoint or proposal) ranks above ordinary informational WATCH items but below every genuine failure/urgency signal (`FAILED_ACTIONS=5`, `PENDING_ACTIONS=10`, `ROUTINE_FAILED=15`, `INTEGRATION_ERROR=20`), exactly matching "genuinely urgent NOW items and safety/failure states remain highest priority." A pin that's already NOW/NEXT on its own real-world merits gets only a small in-category nudge (`_PIN_PRIORITY_NUDGE = 2`) — it can never cross into a different category or leapfrog a more urgent unpinned item. Verified with two direct ordering tests: a genuinely failed (unpinned) action proposal ranks ahead of a pinned, non-urgent BUILD checkpoint; a genuinely failed (unpinned) routine run ranks ahead of a pinned WATCH-tier item too.

**API** (`app/routers/mission_focus.py`, a new route family, plus `mission_focus` embedded directly in `GET /api/briefing/home`'s existing response rather than requiring a second fetch to render Home): `GET /api/mission-focus`, `POST /api/mission-focus/pins`, `PUT /api/mission-focus/pins/{id}` (metadata only — next_action/target_at/blocker; there is no code path from this endpoint to any underlying source row), `POST /api/mission-focus/pins/{id}/unpin`, `PUT /api/mission-focus/reorder`. None of these are Phase 8 proposals — pinning/unpinning/editing/reordering only ever change this module's own presentation/prioritization state, exactly the same reasoning already established for Phase 10A/10B/12A/12B's own local-configuration endpoints.

**Frontend** (`frontend/src/components/MissionFocusRail.tsx`, `AddToMissionFocusButton.tsx`, `views/Home.tsx` extended, plus a restrained "Add to Mission Focus" control wired into `DomainView.tsx`'s structured-record list — life_task/path_deadline/build_checkpoint rows only — `ActionsCentre.tsx`'s pending/approved proposal rows, and `IntegrationsCentre.tsx`'s Calendar event list): the rail sits below the situational briefing as its own compact ledger — rank badge, title, next action, domain chip, target date, blocker (shown only when present), and the same change-state badge vocabulary D87 already established (a shared `components/changeState.ts` module keeps the label mapping in exactly one place) — never a grid of large cards. The default view shows the top 3 ranked pins; the remaining up to 2 sit behind a closed-by-default `<details>` disclosure, matching the exact pattern already established for Phase 12B's acknowledge/snooze history and Addendum 2's `.builder-surface`. Every unified-feed item that's also pinned gets one small, separate `PINNED` badge (violet, matching the core's own accent color) distinct from the change-state badge — never conflated with it. "View source" and "Remove from focus" are two explicit, separate actions per row (never nested inside a single navigable button, since a `<button>` cannot legally contain another interactive control); a source with no `link_target` (unavailable) correctly disables "View source" rather than navigating nowhere silently. "Discuss Mission Focus with Jarvis" is the only path anywhere in this feature that can reach a model — a fresh general conversation, the exact same `createGeneralConversation`/`sendTurn` pattern D86/D87 already established, sending each pin's rank/domain/title/next-action/blocker/target/resolution-state as quoted text through the existing turn endpoint, disabled outright when there are zero pins.

**A real defect found and fixed during this pass, the same class as D86's**: `IntegrationsCentre.tsx`'s new `fetchMissionFocus()` call (added to its existing `Promise.allSettled` batch) read `.active_pins` off the settled value without a fallback; this container's stray already-known port-8000 listener (documented in D86) let an unmocked call in three pre-existing test files resolve with `{}` instead of rejecting, producing `undefined` and crashing three unrelated tests. Fixed with `?? []` at all three call sites (`DomainView.tsx`, `ActionsCentre.tsx`, `IntegrationsCentre.tsx`) and, for determinism going forward, `fetchMissionFocus` is now explicitly mocked in every pre-existing test file that renders one of these three screens — matching the established convention that every Home-adjacent network call stays mocked in shared test suites.

**Container browser QA**: the same temporary Playwright/Chromium harness pattern (`/tmp`, never added to `frontend/package.json`), fictional fixtures covering all five pins (mixed change states, a blocker, a target date, and one genuinely `available: false` source), at 1440×900/1280×800/1024×768/820×900. Zero horizontal overflow; zero console errors; the orbit and the Mission Focus rail both confirmed visible with no vertical overlap at every viewport; a real click on "View source" navigating to LIFE; a real click on "Remove from focus" reaching the (fixture) backend; "Discuss Mission Focus with Jarvis" producing a visibly labeled fixture reply; a logical keyboard-Tab order through Discuss → each row's View source/Remove from focus; `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` re-confirmed to hold ring rotation at `"none"`. A zero-violation axe-core (`wcag2a`+`wcag2aa`) sweep was run with the "2 more pinned" disclosure deliberately expanded first.

**Boundary respected**: no routine was enabled or had its recorded schedule values changed; Hermes toolsets, OAuth, credentials, and real provider accounts were never touched; no real personal data was used anywhere (fictional fixtures and an injected clock throughout every backend and frontend test, and in the container QA pass); no sub-agent, Hermes skill, hook, memory, or cron was introduced; MIND/PEOPLE remain structurally unreadable by this feature (both the direct validation path and a bypass-the-validation defense-in-depth test); no Git operation was performed; no model call happens anywhere in pin creation, editing, reordering, unpinning, or briefing assembly — verified by the same AST-based import/call-site check D86/D87 already established, now covering `mission_focus_service.py` too.

**Why**: merging pin metadata into the exact same candidate/fingerprint/ledger machinery Phase 12B already built — rather than adding a parallel "pinned items" list with its own priority and change-tracking logic — is what makes every one of Mission Focus's continuity guarantees (false-resolution protection, acknowledge/snooze compatibility, deterministic ranking against genuine urgency) true by construction instead of by careful parallel reimplementation. Real database-level enforcement of the five-pin invariant (partial unique indexes plus triggers, not just an application-level count check) is what makes "prevent races that bypass the five-pin limit" an actually-verified property rather than an assumption about single-threaded request handling that could quietly stop being true.

**How to apply**: any future Mission-Focus-eligible source type must follow the exact same three-part pattern — add it to `MISSION_FOCUS_SOURCE_TYPES`, teach `resolve_pin_source()` how to resolve it (title/category/tone/domain/fingerprint fields, bypassing any ephemeral inclusion window that source's normal candidate-gathering applies), and confirm its domain can never resolve to MIND/PEOPLE (or add an explicit check if it ever could, exactly like `action_proposal`'s). Never add a new priority scale, a new fingerprint function, or a new suppression mechanism for a pinned item — reuse the existing ones exactly as `_merge_pin_into_item`/`_synthesize_pin_item` already do.

**Verification**: backend 611/611 (547 + 64 new: `test_migration_0014.py` 2, `test_mission_focus_service.py` 22, `test_mission_focus_briefing.py` 23, `test_mission_focus_api.py` 14, `test_mission_focus_export_restore.py` 3 — zero real Google/Keychain/Hermes/model contact throughout). Frontend 218/218 (200 + 18 new: `Home.missionFocus.test.tsx` 12, `AddToMissionFocusButton.test.tsx` 6), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories, confirmed by an explicit before/after category count — identical to D86/D87's) all pass.

## D89 — First real-Mac transition/acceptance pass: two genuine defects found (top-bar Hermes-degraded color, and a naive-datetime round-trip through SQLite), migrations 0011-0014 applied to the real installation, real-data acceptance for Phases 6/9/10A/12A/12B/12C

**Context**: the first session with actual access to Bernardo's real Mac, real `~/JarvisData`, real Hermes gateway, real Keychain-backed Google Calendar/Health connections, and the real production build. Confirmed genuinely running on the real Mac (not a container) via `uname` (`Darwin arm64`), `pwd`, and the existence of `/Users/bernardoguterrres/Desktop/Jarvis` and `~/JarvisData` with real content. No `.git` directory exists in this project (confirmed, matches §18a) and none was created.

**Backup and export before migrating**: `jarvis-cli backup --category daily` and `jarvis-cli export` were both run against the real installation while it was still at its pre-existing schema revision `0010`, then `jarvis-cli validate` confirmed the export archive (`schema_revision: 0010`, `included_components: ['database']` — `documents/`, `domain-summaries/`, and `skills/` were independently confirmed empty on disk, so "database only" is correct, not a gap). Only after this succeeded were migrations applied.

**Migrations applied through the normal launcher, not raw Alembic**: `scripts/jarvisctl.sh open` (which itself calls `alembic upgrade head` internally) carried the real database from `0010` to `0014` — Phase 6's general-conversation nullable-domain migration and Phases 12A/12B/12C's briefing/continuity/Mission-Focus tables — with no manual migration-file edits. Confirmed via `alembic current` before (`0010`) and after (`0014 (head)`).

**Defect 1 — top-bar "Jarvis: unavailable"/"Jarvis: model not configured" used the critical (red) `.health-dot.error` class instead of the degraded (amber) treatment** (`frontend/src/App.tsx`): the exact defect the container report (D77-D80) flagged as still needing real-Mac confirmation. `ModelLinkBanner` already correctly used amber for "Hermes down, backend up" (a `tone="degraded"`, per `Diagnostic.tsx`'s own documented color contract: `critical` = actually unavailable, `degraded` = reduced but usable), but the top-bar dot's `agentStatusClass` collapsed both "Hermes unavailable" and "model not configured" into `"error"` (red) — the same treatment reserved for a genuinely unavailable controller. Fixed by changing `agentStatusClass`'s fallback branch from `"error"` to a new `"degraded"` class (`frontend/src/index.css`, same amber `--status-warn` token `ModelLinkBanner` already uses) — red now stays reserved for `ControllerOfflineDiagnostic`'s own domain. Two focused regression tests added to `App.test.tsx` first (Hermes unavailable, and Hermes-up-but-no-model), both failing against the old code and passing against the fix; full frontend suite 220/220, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories) all pass.

**Defect 2 — a genuine, previously-undetected timezone bug: `acknowledge_item`/`snooze_item` returned naive datetimes after a SQLite round-trip, which the API then serialized without a UTC offset, which the browser's `new Date(iso)` then misread as local time** (`backend/app/briefing_service.py`). SQLAlchemy's `DateTime(timezone=True)` is not actually honored by the SQLite dialect — a value written as aware UTC comes back naive (no `tzinfo`) after any `session.refresh()`/fresh query, a fact the codebase already knew and guarded against elsewhere with an `_as_aware()` normalizer (`routine_service.py` has the identical helper, used for `next_due_at`), but `acknowledge_item`, `snooze_item`, and `_acknowledged_and_snoozed_entries` (the function `GET /api/briefing/home`'s `acknowledged_and_snoozed` list actually goes through) never applied it. This was invisible in every prior container test run because the container's system clock is UTC, so a naive value misread as "local" happened to equal the correct UTC value by coincidence — it only surfaced now because the real Mac's system timezone is BST (UTC+1): `uv run pytest` failed one pre-existing test, `test_snooze_tomorrow_morning_uses_requested_timezone` (expected local hour 8, got 7 — exactly a one-hour BST offset error). Root-caused (not worked around) by tracing the exact SQLite round-trip, confirmed in isolation with a small REPL reproduction, and cross-checked against `routine_service._as_aware`'s existing, already-tested pattern for the identical defect class. Fixed by normalizing the in-memory ORM attribute immediately after `session.refresh()` in both `acknowledge_item` and `snooze_item` (`ack.acknowledged_at = _as_aware(...)`, `snooze.snoozed_at`/`snooze.snooze_until = _as_aware(...)`), and by wrapping the three datetime fields `_acknowledged_and_snoozed_entries` reads back from fresh queries the same way — matching the codebase's own established convention rather than inventing a new one. A new regression test, `test_snooze_and_acknowledge_timestamps_survive_db_round_trip_as_aware_utc`, directly exercises the actual API-facing path (`_acknowledged_and_snoozed_entries`, not just the lower-level `_tomorrow_morning_utc` helper the pre-existing test covers) and was confirmed to fail without the fix (verified by temporarily reverting it) and pass with it. Full backend suite 612/612 (611 + 1 new). This was not one of the enumerated known items going into this pass — it was found only because real-Mac acceptance ran the existing suite under a genuinely non-UTC system clock, exactly the class of defect a UTC-only container can structurally never catch.

**Real-data acceptance performed** (read-only checks against the real, already-connected installation; no credential, schedule, OAuth, or routine state was changed): all six domains present via `GET /api/domains`; `GET /api/agent/status` reports `hermes_available: true`, `model_configured: true`, `model: "openai-codex/gpt-5.6-terra"` (matches CLAUDE.md §10's currently-configured model); `GET /api/integrations` shows both Google Calendar and Google Health `"status": "connected"` with correct, non-overlapping, provider-specific scopes (no scope bleed — matches D62-D65's correction); automatic resync schedules preserved exactly as D69 recorded them (Calendar every 15 minutes, Google Health every 60 minutes, both `enabled: true`, both `last_status: "ok"`); all three Phase 10B routines confirmed still `enabled: false` with their original never-yet-activated default schedule values — correctly untouched, since enabling them requires Bernardo's explicit approval per CLAUDE.md §9/§12, not this session's; real conversations (2 BODY, 1 general), real action-proposal history (2, matching the Phase 9 Calendar create/delete acceptance), and real integration data all survived the 0010→0014 migration and a full backend restart intact. `GET /api/briefing/home` against real data returned a genuinely empty `items: []` with truthful `"ok"` source statuses (independently confirmed LIFE/PATH/BUILD have zero structured records right now, so the empty result is correct, not a bug) and `mission_focus: []` (no pins yet) — this exercises Phase 12A/12B/12C's real-data wiring structurally but cannot yet demonstrate a genuine new/changed/resolved transition, since there's no real LIFE/PATH/BUILD/Mission-Focus content to change; that specific observation remains a "requires several real days" item exactly as the Roadmap already said. A real-browser check against the actual production build (via `claude-in-chrome`, not a fixture harness) confirmed Home renders all six domains, the orbit, a truthful "No immediate items" briefing strip, and a "Mission Focus (0/5)" rail with no console errors; the Systems popover and Integrations Centre were confirmed rendering real Calendar connection status, scopes disclosure, and automatic-sync controls correctly against real data.

**Process/security posture reconfirmed live**: exactly one logical backend process (the `uv run uvicorn` wrapper plus its one child, matching the exact pattern Phase 7's own acceptance already established as "one backend"); both the Hermes gateway (`127.0.0.1:8642`) and the Jarvis backend (`127.0.0.1:8000`) confirmed bound to loopback only via `lsof`; the authenticated `GET /v1/toolsets` call (bearer token read from `backend/.env` and used only inline, never printed) confirmed 27 total toolsets, 0 enabled; `backend.log` scanned for token-shaped strings (JWT/`ya29.`/40+ char hex/`Bearer <token>` patterns) with zero matches — the only `access_token` occurrences found were Python source lines from a stack trace (the literal variable name in code), not a printed value.

**Not performed, and correctly left to Bernardo per CLAUDE.md's manual-setup/hardware boundary**: reading real Keychain values directly via the `security` CLI (blocked by this session's own permission classifier as a raw credential-store access — reasonable; connection status already confirms the Keychain-backed credentials load and work correctly at sync time, which is the practical proxy this session can verify); one real push-to-talk question from Home with real microphone/TTS hardware; one real confirm-required voice command (e.g. "export data"); a manual VoiceOver pass; observing genuine briefing new/changed/resolved transitions over several real days; activating Phase 10B's three routines (Bernardo's schedule selections remain recorded and unapplied, exactly as before this pass).

**Boundary respected**: no Git operation was performed (no `.git` exists, none created); no routine was enabled or had its schedule changed; no OAuth consent, credential, or provider permission was touched; no Hermes tool/agent/delegation/cron/browser/terminal/filesystem toolset was enabled (still 0/27); the pre-migration backup and export were created and validated before any schema change touched the real database.

**Why**: a UTC-only container can never surface a bug whose entire failure mode is "naive datetime misread as local time," no matter how thorough its test suite — this is a structural blind spot, not a gap in test-writing discipline. Running the existing, already-passing suite on a machine with a genuinely different system timezone is what turned a latent, silently-wrong production behavior (every "Hidden until"/acknowledged timestamp shown to Bernardo, off by his UTC offset) into a caught, fixed defect before it ever reached him through the UI.

**How to apply**: any future datetime read back from a SQLAlchemy `DateTime(timezone=True)` column on SQLite — in `briefing_service.py`, `routine_service.py`, or any future module — must be wrapped in that module's `_as_aware()` helper before being used in `.astimezone()`, `.isoformat()`, or handed to a Pydantic response field; never assume a value that was aware before `session.commit()`/`session.refresh()` is still aware afterward.
