When a future phase adds new acknowledge/snooze/pin-style timestamp fields, apply the same immediately-after-refresh normalization this fix established, rather than only fixing the specific field a test happens to catch.

**Verification**: backend 612/612 (611 + 1 new regression test). Frontend 220/220 (218 + 2 new regression tests), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories) all pass. Real installation restarted cleanly after both fixes (`jarvisctl.sh stop` then `open`); `alembic current` reconfirmed at `0014 (head)`; all six domains, both integration connections and their schedules, and exactly one backend process reconfirmed healthy post-restart.

## D90 — Home orbital HUD: removed a rectangular panel behind the Jarvis core, replaced the domain-navigation delay with a real shared-element View Transition, and calmed the hover/activation motion

**Context**: real-Mac visual inspection of the production build (same session as D89) found a visible rectangular surface behind the central `JarvisCore` ring assembly — clearest while a domain like BUILD was focused, since the square's border tracked `--ring-color`. Separately, Bernardo's own report identified that clicking a domain node made the circle visibly grow, paused, then abruptly swapped Home for the destination screen — reading as input lag rather than intentional motion. Both are frontend-only, no backend/data/credential/routine/Hermes/Git changes.

**Root cause of the square, found by inspecting live computed styles (not guessed from source alone)**: `.jarvis-hud-interactive` (`JarvisCore.tsx`'s button, when the core is a real "Talk to Jarvis" control) already reset its own `background`/`border`/`padding` to nothing — but a higher-specificity global rule, `button:not(.primary):not(.back-button):not(.domain-button):not(.push-to-talk-button)` (`index.css`), never listed `.jarvis-hud-interactive` in its exclusion set, so its rectangular panel background/border/border-radius still won the cascade on top of the reset. `getComputedStyle()` on the live button confirmed `background: rgb(24,21,40)`, `border: 1px solid rgb(52,44,77)`, `borderRadius: 6px` before the fix, and fully transparent/borderless/radius-0 after adding `:not(.jarvis-hud-interactive)` to all three variants of that rule (base/`:hover`/`:active`) — the exact same exclusion pattern `.domain-button` and `.push-to-talk-button` already used, which is why neither of those had this bug. Confirmed visually (zoomed screenshot before/after) and with a new CSS-source regression test (`homeCoreStyling.test.ts`) that fails if the exclusion is ever removed.

**Navigation delay, root cause and fix**: `Home.tsx`'s `handleSelect` wrapped every domain click in `setTimeout(() => onSelectDomain(slug), 220)` (plus a second, independent 220ms `setTimeout` in `App.tsx`'s `navigateToTarget` for typed/spoken domain commands, and a `.domain-node.is-focusing .domain-button { transform: scale(1.14); }` — a 14% grow, well past "restrained") — three separate places manually simulating the "energize before navigating" feel the design brief actually wanted from a single continuous morph. Both `setTimeout` calls were removed outright (no replacement delay of any kind); the 1.14 scale was reduced to 1.03 (within the requested 1.02-1.04 band) with its own short (90ms) transition so the activation ring still reads as an intentional, immediate signal rather than nothing at all.

**Shared View Transition** (`frontend/src/transitions/domainViewTransition.ts`, new module): `runDomainViewTransition(slug, commit)` is the one function every Home→domain path now calls — pointer click and keyboard activation (`DomainNode`'s existing `onClick`/`onFocus` wiring, unchanged), number shortcuts (`App.tsx`'s digit-key handler), and a typed/spoken command naming a domain while Home is visible (`navigateToTarget`'s `domain:` branch) — replacing three independent ad-hoc implementations with one. It tags whichever single DOM node currently carries `data-domain-transition-slug="<slug>"` with one shared `view-transition-name` before calling `document.startViewTransition()`, runs `commit` via `flushSync` (so the DOM swap is synchronous within the transition's capture window, matching the standard React 18 + View Transitions integration pattern), then tags whichever node carries that same data attribute afterward — Home's node going in, the new `DomainEmblem` in the domain view's own header (`frontend/src/components/DomainEmblem.tsx`, new — a small, ~34px, non-animated circular badge reusing the existing kbd-number convention, added to `DomainView.tsx`'s header) going out, and the reverse for "Back to Jarvis" (`App.tsx`'s new `goHomeFromDomainWithTransition`, using the exact same function with `slug`/`commit` swapped). When no matching node exists on either side — a command fired from a Centre page, where Home was never mounted — no name is ever assigned, so the browser's own default root crossfade covers that case exactly as the "clean short page crossfade" fallback the brief asked for, with no separate code path required. Falls back to an immediate, undelayed `commit()` (never an artificial wait either way) when `document.startViewTransition` doesn't exist or `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is set — the JS layer bypasses the API entirely in both cases, so the reduced-motion guarantee is structural, not merely a CSS override. A module-level in-flight latch (`transitionInFlight`) makes a second activation a no-op until the first's `commit` has genuinely run, since `startViewTransition`'s callback runs in a microtask rather than synchronously, leaving a real (if brief) window for a rapid double-click/double-Enter to otherwise reach this function twice.

**Choreography CSS** (`index.css`, all guarded under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`): `::view-transition-group(jarvis-domain-shared)` gets a 420ms duration on the project's existing `--ease-hud` cinematic curve for the morph itself; `::view-transition-old(root)`/`::view-transition-new(root)` get a 260ms duration, with the default new-root fade-in keyframe replaced by `jarvis-domain-reveal` (opacity 0→1 plus an 8px upward translate, `animation-delay: 90ms` so the destination page's own reveal visibly follows the identity morph rather than racing it) — this single default-root customization is what makes "the other five nodes, the orbit path, and the core all dim/fade together" true for free, since they're simply the old root snapshot fading out, with no bespoke per-node dimming animation needed. A `.no-view-transitions` class (added once at startup in `main.tsx` via a plain `typeof document.startViewTransition !== "function"` check) gates a small fallback `jarvis-domain-reveal` crossfade for browsers without the API at all — still no JS timing, purely a CSS animation riding the natural remount.

**Hover calming** (task-requested, `index.css`): `--motion-ring-enter`/`--motion-ring-exit` (the two-ring hover treatment's fade durations) tightened from 300ms/200ms to 260ms/190ms (both now inside the requested 220-280ms/170-220ms bands); the ring's dormant rest scale raised from `0.88` to `0.96` so its entrance reads as a fade rather than a noticeable grow. Home's existing 100ms pointer hover-intent delay (`HOVER_INTENT_MS`) was already within the requested 70-100ms band and needed no change; it was never gating actual click/keyboard activation, which remains immediate.

**A genuine, environment-specific artifact found and understood, not silently ignored**: after the fix, a real (CDP-automated) Chrome tab logged `InvalidStateError: Transition was aborted because of invalid state` on every domain transition — reproduced identically with a completely empty `document.startViewTransition(() => {})`, and traced to `document.visibilityState === "hidden"` / `document.hidden === true` on that automated tab (confirmed by direct inspection), a documented condition under which Chrome's View Transitions API rejects its `ready` promise regardless of any application code. `updateCallbackDone` and `finished` both resolved normally throughout — the actual DOM swap always completed correctly; only the custom morph animation itself was skipped in this specific automation context. Added `transition.ready.catch(() => {})` regardless, since any consumer should handle that promise defensively — verified with a dedicated test (`domainViewTransition.test.ts`) that fails with a real unhandled-rejection error without the fix and passes with it. **Confirming the actual smooth shared-element morph plays with zero console noise still requires a normal, human-focused browser tab** (where `document.hidden` is false) — this is the same class of "genuinely real-hardware/interactive-only" limitation already established for microphone/TTS/OAuth checks (D72/D78), now applying to View Transitions specifically.

**Verification**: 15 new frontend tests (`domainViewTransition.test.ts` 7, `Home.transition.test.tsx` 4, `homeCoreStyling.test.ts` 5 — square-free structure, restrained activation scale, reduced-motion CSS guard structure, no-delay click/keyboard navigation, double-activation protection, reverse navigation, unsupported-API fallback, and the `ready`-rejection regression), each independently confirmed to fail without its corresponding fix and pass with it; full frontend suite 236/236, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (zero new warning categories, confirmed by an explicit before/after count) all pass. Live-verified against the real production build in a real (if automation-controlled) Chrome tab: the square is gone at idle (zoomed screenshot, before/after), a domain click navigates immediately with the new header emblem correctly present, "Back to Jarvis" correctly returns to Home with all six domains, and the Hermes-degraded/model-configured top-bar states from D89 are unaffected. No backend, data, credential, schedule, OAuth, routine, or Hermes configuration was touched; no Git operation was performed.

## D91 — DomainGlyph: a bespoke six-icon family replacing the plain first-letter mark, a real domain-numbering defect found and fixed, glyphs promoted into Home nodes, and a redundant status indicator demoted to conditional

**Context**: Bernardo asked for the plain B/M/P/P/B/L first-letter marks inside the small circular domain emblems (D90's `DomainEmblem.tsx`) to become a bespoke family of six inline-SVG glyphs, then — after reviewing the first rendered pass — asked for a numbering-consistency fix, several glyph-concept refinements, promoting the glyph into the dominant visual on each Home node, reusing D90's existing shared-element transition rather than building a second one, and reconsidering a second circular symbol (`MiniCoreIndicator`) sitting beside the new domain emblem. This entry covers the whole arc as one unit, not two — the numbering defect was found *during* the icon work, not a separately-scoped fix.

**The icon family** (`frontend/src/components/DomainGlyph.tsx`, new): one 24×24-viewBox, `currentColor`, ~1.6px-rounded-stroke, primarily-outline construction shared by all six — never a generated PNG/favicon, emoji, or external icon library. Each concept went through at least one real rendered-size correction before settling, because several genuinely didn't read at 20-24px on first pass despite looking fine as isolated path data:
- **BODY** — a small head circle plus a heartbeat/pulse trace; the one concept that was correct on the first pass and never revised.
- **MIND** — a minimal symmetric brain outline (two merged hemispheres, a center division, two short fold strokes). The first attempt (four curved "waves" converging on a center dot, meant to suggest neural/thought activity abstractly) tested as ambiguous — Bernardo's own read was "ambiguous spark/neural mark" — so it was replaced with an actual brain silhouette, which is unmistakable at any size tested.
- **PEOPLE** — two minimal overlapping figures (head circle + shoulder arc, the nearer one larger). Two earlier attempts were both rejected by direct inspection before this one: a three-node "share/network" triangle (literally the generic icon this whole feature exists to avoid) came first; replacing it with two embracing arcs plus a center dot then collapsed into an unreadable single ring-and-dot at 20px, which was caught by zooming into the actual rendered node, not by reading the path data.
- **PATH** — an ascending route from a filled origin waypoint to an explicit arrowhead, replacing an earlier version that ended in a second plain circle (a "two dots and a line" shape Bernardo correctly flagged as too generic to read as trajectory/direction).
- **BUILD** — three rounded modules overlapping directly in a staircase (no connector lines). An earlier version used three separate squares joined by thin diagonal connector lines in a triangular arrangement, which risked reading as an org chart; removing the lines and letting the modules' own outlines overlap reads as assembly instead.
- **LIFE** — a compass: a ring, a slim directional needle through the center, and two side markers. Two earlier attempts were rejected: four short tick marks around a ring tested as too subtle to register as anything at 20px, and the next attempt (a plain circle with a full cross through it, plus one small offset triangle) was correctly flagged as reading like a crosshair/target rather than a compass — the needle shape is what actually disambiguates "compass" from "aim reticle" or the originally-avoided refresh/sync arrow.

**Purely decorative, structurally**: every glyph is `aria-hidden="true"`, `focusable="false"`, carries no `aria-label`, and its own rendered text content is empty (verified by a direct test) — the enclosing control's real accessible name (a domain button's `aria-label`, a domain view's `<h1>`) is the only source of truth, so the icon can never duplicate or drift out of sync with it. Renders nothing for an unrecognized slug rather than guessing.

**A real defect found while wiring this in, not a pre-planned fix**: Home's orbital badges number domains by whatever order `GET /api/domains` actually returns (`order_by(Domain.slug)` in `app/routers/domains.py` — alphabetical: body, build, life, mind, path, people), but the header emblem (`DomainEmblem.tsx`) and the `1`-`6` keyboard shortcut handler (`App.tsx`) each carried their own separate hardcoded array in the *original* CLAUDE.md narrative order (body, mind, people, path, build, life) — a leftover from before Home's numbering was ever tied to the live API response. The result, exactly as Bernardo reported it: MIND showed "4" on Home but "2" in its own header, and — a more serious consequence neither of us had separately noticed — pressing the "2" key actually opened MIND while Home visibly labeled BUILD as "2". Root-caused by tracing both arrays back to `backend/app/routers/domains.py`'s actual sort order, not by guessing which one was "right."

**Fix — one authoritative mapping, not three arrays kept in sync by hand**: `frontend/src/domainOrder.ts` (new) declares `DOMAIN_SLUG_ORDER = ["body", "build", "life", "mind", "path", "people"]` once, plus `domainNumber(slug)`/`domainSlugForNumber(n)` (both return `null` for anything outside the real six, never a guessed number). `App.tsx`'s digit-shortcut handler, `DomainEmblem.tsx`'s header badge, and `DomainNode.tsx`'s Home badge (previously blindly using its array-position `index`, now explicitly calling `domainNumber(domain.slug)` so it's no longer only *coincidentally* correct by matching the API's sort order) all read from this one module. `DomainGlyph.tsx` also imports the same `DOMAIN_SLUG_ORDER`/`DomainSlug` type rather than declaring its own fourth copy. A new regression test (`domainNumbering.test.tsx`) renders the real `App`, and for each of the six domains asserts Home's badge, the destination header's badge, and the keyboard shortcut that opens it all agree — confirmed to genuinely fail (a `ReferenceError` inside `DomainView`, not just a wrong number) when a separate hardcoded array is reintroduced anywhere in the chain, proving the test exercises the real integration rather than three independently-correct assertions.

**The glyph promoted into Home's own nodes**: `DomainNode.tsx` now renders the same `DomainGlyph` as the dominant central visual, sized via `calc(var(--node-size) * 0.32)` (so it scales with the node rather than a fixed pixel value that could clip on a narrower viewport) rather than a fixed 36-44px. Color follows the existing hover(violet)/focus(violet)/`.is-focusing`(cyan-with-glow) vocabulary the button already had — no new color scale invented for the icon. The visual hierarchy stays core → glyph → name → subtitle → shortcut number; the Jarvis core's own size/detail was untouched, so it remains unambiguously the largest, most detailed element on Home.

**The transition reused verbatim, not rebuilt**: no change was needed to `transitions/domainViewTransition.ts` itself — the shared-element target was already "whichever element carries `data-domain-transition-slug`" (D90), and that element's *content* (a letter, now a glyph) was always irrelevant to the transition mechanism. Adding the glyph inside both the Home button and the header emblem means the glyph itself is what visibly travels during the morph, for free.

**A redundant status indicator, demoted rather than deleted**: `MiniCoreIndicator` (a small violet arc + dot) sat beside the domain-header emblem showing "Jarvis is idle/active" — once the emblem carried a real glyph, an always-visible second circular symbol at rest read as competing decoration. It does convey a real, real-only-sometimes-true state (`sendingToJarvis || voiceState !== "idle"`), so it was never deleted outright: `DomainView.tsx` now only mounts it at all while that's genuinely true, so an idle header shows exactly one identity mark, and the indicator still appears the moment there's something real to signal. A dedicated test (`DomainView.test.tsx`) asserts `.mini-core` is absent at idle and present (still `size="sm"`) only while a turn is genuinely in flight.

**Verification**: 4 new frontend test files/additions (`domainOrder.test.ts` 4, `domainNumbering.test.tsx` 2, `components/DomainGlyph.test.tsx` 5, plus 2 new cases in `DomainView.test.tsx`), each confirmed against a real reverted-fix run to fail without its corresponding correction; full frontend suite 249/249, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (13 warnings, all pre-existing categories, zero new) all pass. Live-verified against the real production build in a real (if automation-controlled) Chrome tab at the default window size: canonical numbering (BODY 1, BUILD 2, LIFE 3, MIND 4, PATH 5, PEOPLE 6) confirmed identical on Home and every domain header; MIND unmistakably reads as a brain, LIFE unmistakably reads as a compass (not a crosshair, not refresh/sync) after their respective corrections; the idle header shows exactly one circular identity mark. **Not independently re-confirmed this pass**: the exact 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoints specifically — this session's browser-automation `resize_window` call did not change the tab's actual rendered viewport, a tool limitation rather than a skipped check; the changes made (proportional glyph sizing off existing size tokens, no new fixed-width elements, no layout restructuring) are not the kind that would plausibly introduce new overflow against the already browser-QA-verified responsive layout from D71-D80, but this should be re-confirmed with real viewport control before being called fully closed. No backend, data, credential, schedule, OAuth, routine, or Hermes configuration was touched; no Git operation was performed.

## D92 — DomainGlyph visual-refinement pass against a supplied reference image, and a size-aware `variant` prop

**Context**: Bernardo supplied a ChatGPT-generated moodboard PNG as a visual-direction reference (explicitly proportions/hierarchy/line-weight/placement — never a raster asset to place in the app or trace edges from) and, having seen D91's glyphs rendered live, asked for four of the six concepts to be pushed further, the D91 numbering/`domainOrder.ts` architecture to remain completely untouched, and a size-aware mechanism so a glyph could carry a little more detail at Home's larger node without cluttering the small header emblem. The reference image's own clockwise arrangement (BODY top, BUILD upper-right, LIFE lower-right, MIND bottom, PATH lower-left, PEOPLE upper-left) was checked against Home's actual layout and found to already match exactly — no positional change was needed.

**BODY and BUILD — concept confirmed, not revised.** BODY's plain vitals waveform was already correct; it was only tightened into an explicit five-segment/central-peak/terminal-dot shape (no head, torso, heart, or medical-cross shape, matching the explicit instruction). BUILD's three overlapping modules were already correct in concept; two small joint-accent dots were added at the overlaps (`variant="home"` only) for a more technical/circuit feel without adding fine detail that would vanish at header size.

**MIND — from symmetric hemispheres to an organic side-profile.** D91's brain outline was a real, recognizable brain, but its two-hemisphere symmetry was correctly flagged as reading like a front-facing medical/pharma logo rather than a side/three-quarter profile. Replaced with a single asymmetric, organic silhouette (no center dividing line) plus two short internal fold strokes, the second of which is `variant="home"`-only.

**PEOPLE — an explicit connecting arc added.** D91's two overlapping figures already read as "people," but nothing in the mark stated *connection* beyond their proximity. A single subtle arc now joins the space above the two heads, directly matching "two minimal human figures... connected by one subtle shared arc."

**PATH — from an arrow-tipped curve to three explicit milestones and a beacon.** D91's PATH ended in a small arrowhead, which risked reading as GPS/driving-navigation direction rather than career/education/deadline progression — exactly the one thing the brief said to avoid. Replaced with three straight ascending segments through three explicit filled waypoint dots, ending in a small four-point beacon/sparkle glint (`variant="home"` renders the sparkle's four short ticks; `variant="header"` collapses it to a single dot, so the destination marker never fully disappears at small size).

**LIFE — from a compass needle to a calendar-with-orbit.** D91's compass needle successfully stopped reading as sync/refresh/reload (the specific defect it was built to fix), but it reduced LIFE to "direction," not the domain's actual breadth (calendar, tasks, finances, housing, travel, purchases, administration). Replaced with a small rounded calendar panel (two top hanger-tabs, `variant="home"`-only internal date-row ticks) with one open elliptical arc sweeping around it — deliberately never closed into a full ring and never given an arrowhead, so it can't be mistaken for sync/refresh, and deliberately not a crosshair (no straight cross-spokes of any kind, the exact shape that read as a targeting reticle earlier in D91's own iteration).

**The `variant` prop — a narrow, tested escape hatch, not two icon sets.** `DomainGlyph` gained `variant?: "home" | "header"` (default `"header"`, matching `DomainEmblem.tsx`'s existing call site unchanged; `DomainNode.tsx` now explicitly passes `variant="home"`). Only MIND/PATH/BUILD/LIFE use it, each hiding exactly one or two fine strokes that independent rendered-size inspection showed added clutter rather than clarity at ~20px — never a different primary shape. Three new tests enforce this structurally rather than by convention: `"header"` is the true default (byte-identical to an explicit `variant="header"` call), `"home"` never renders fewer shapes than `"header"` for any of the six domains, and both variants share the same `viewBox` and the same first/primary shape element for every domain.

**The Home-node glyph size was nudged up** (`--node-size` multiplier `0.32` → `0.36`, `index.css`) so the largest supported node size (118px) puts the glyph at ~42px, inside the requested 38-46px band — D91's `0.32` landed at the low end (38px) of that band, not below it, but the correction gives a small additional margin toward "dominant."

**Not independently re-confirmed this pass, same limitation as D91**: the exact 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoints — this session's browser-automation `resize_window` call again did not change the tab's actual rendered viewport (a tool limitation, not a skipped check); at the default rendered viewport, `document.body.scrollWidth === document.body.clientWidth` (no overflow) held throughout, and none of this pass's changes (path-data edits to existing glyph shapes, a same-size-token multiplier tweak, a purely additive/conditional `variant` prop) are of a kind that would plausibly introduce new overflow.

**Verification**: 3 new tests in `components/DomainGlyph.test.tsx` (8 total, up from 5), each exercising the `variant` contract described above; the full domain-numbering/transition/reduced-motion/header-identity test surface from D91 (`domainOrder.test.ts`, `domainNumbering.test.tsx`, `domainViewTransition.test.ts`, `Home.transition.test.tsx`, `homeCoreStyling.test.ts`, `DomainView.test.tsx`) re-run and still passing unchanged, since none of that architecture was touched; full frontend suite 252/252, `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (13 warnings, all pre-existing categories, zero new) all pass. Backend test suite was deliberately not re-run — no backend file changed. Live-verified against the real production build in a real (if automation-controlled) Chrome tab: canonical numbering unchanged and still consistent everywhere; MIND now reads as an organic brain silhouette rather than a symmetric medical mark; PEOPLE's connecting arc visible at both sizes; PATH's three waypoints and beacon visible at Home size, collapsing gracefully at header size; BUILD's joint accents visible at Home size and cleanly absent (not a smear) at header size; LIFE reads as a calendar-with-orbit at both sizes, confirmed to not resemble a crosshair or a refresh/sync arrow; a reduced-motion `matchMedia` stub confirmed a domain navigation completes instantly with zero console output (the JS bypasses `document.startViewTransition` entirely, exactly as D90 established); zero console errors during this pass's own navigation clicks. No backend, data, credential, schedule, OAuth, routine, or Hermes configuration was touched; no Git operation was performed.

## D93 — Focused re-refinement of PEOPLE, PATH, MIND, and LIFE, using a temporary in-app comparison surface; a real "reads as an eye" defect found and fixed

**Context**: after seeing D92's PEOPLE/PATH/MIND/LIFE rendered live, Bernardo judged all four not good enough — PEOPLE read as a generic restroom/group pictogram, PATH read as a bone/branch/wand rather than progression, MIND read as a cloud or flower rather than a brain, and LIFE's orbit and calendar sat so tightly together that they knotted into an unreadable shape. BODY and BUILD were explicitly out of scope and left completely untouched. `domainOrder.ts`'s architecture, Home/DomainView layout, the shared-element transition, colors, and accessibility were all explicitly required to stay unchanged — this was a geometry-only pass on four glyph functions.

**A temporary, in-app comparison surface, built and removed as instructed.** Rather than a hand-copied static mockup (which risks drifting from real rendering), `frontend/src/GlyphLab.tsx` was added temporarily and wired into `main.tsx` behind `?glyphlab=1` — it reused the real `.domain-button`/`.domain-emblem`/`.domain-node-glyph` classes and CSS, so what it showed was pixel-identical to production, not an approximation. It rendered all six glyphs at both variants with a genuine toggle for the `is-focusing` (selected/cyan) state, real `:hover` (violet) via actual pointer hover, and real `:focus-visible` via actual Tab navigation. This immediately surfaced a real defect neither the written brief nor source review had caught: a fully closed ellipse orbit tightly enclosing a centered calendar rectangle reads unmistakably as **an eye** (sclera + pupil), not a calendar. It also surfaced that a from-scratch bezier attempt at an asymmetric brain outline, built from many small similarly-sized curve segments, reads as a cauliflower/cloud regardless of overall asymmetry — the *number and uniformity* of bumps drives that reading, not just symmetry. Both root causes were found by comparing the actual rendered shape against the described intent, not guessed from path coordinates alone. `GlyphLab.tsx` and its `main.tsx` wiring were removed once the four glyphs were finalized, per the temporary-harness convention already established in D72/D75/D80/D91/D92 (a disposable QA surface, never shipped) — confirmed by the final production build's module count returning to 73 (matching D92's count, down from 74 while the lab existed).

**PEOPLE — smaller, asymmetric single-side shoulder sweeps, not a closed "U" torso.** Each figure is now a head circle plus one open, single-direction curved stroke sweeping away from the head (`M4.4 18.4c-0.3-3.4 1.2-5.8 3.4-6.1`-style paths) rather than a symmetric two-sided arc closing into the exact silhouette a restroom-sign pictogram uses. The two figures are also now visibly different sizes (one head `r=1.7`, one `r=2`) to read as two distinct individuals rather than a matched pictogram pair. The connecting arc above the heads (already added in D92) is unchanged.

**PATH — four waypoints with unmistakable start/end asymmetry, on a smooth continuous route.** A larger filled circular start waypoint, a single smooth multi-segment forward-leaning route (still one continuous stroke, never a single straight segment — a lone straight thick-capped line is exactly what reads as a bone), two smaller intermediate milestone dots, and a distinct filled diamond at the end (a different shape from the start circle, so start and destination are never ambiguous even in silhouette). No arrowhead anywhere, so it can't be mistaken for a generic direction arrow either.

**MIND — one deliberate top cleft, not many uniform bumps, plus a clearly separated hooked stem.** The outline is built from six large, mostly-smooth cubic segments with exactly one intentional dip at the top-center (`...C12 6.4 12.5 6.4 13 5.5C15 5...` — two curves meeting at a shallow local dip between them, read as two lobes) rather than the ~12 small similarly-sized bumps the D92 attempt used, which is what produced the cloud/flower reading regardless of the overall silhouette's asymmetry. A distinct hooked stem (`M12.5 18.9c0.1 1.3-0.5 2.3-1.6 2.7`) hangs visibly below the main mass and is drawn as its own separate stroke, since no cloud, flower, gear, or badge silhouette has anything protruding from its base like this. Two short internal fold strokes (home variant only) sit inside the mass.

**LIFE — an open arc, never a closed loop, sitting entirely above the calendar.** The full ellipse from D92 is replaced with a single open elliptical arc path (`M2.6 11 A9.4 6.8 0 0 1 21.4 11`) spanning only the top ~180° — there is no closed loop of any kind, which is what makes the "eye" reading structurally impossible rather than just visually unlikely. The calendar (unchanged two-tab, one-date-line construction) was moved down (`y=12` instead of `y=9`) so its top edge sits clearly below the arc's endpoints (`y=11`), leaving real negative space between the two shapes at every x-position across the calendar's width — verified algebraically (the calendar's bounding box lies entirely inside the arc's enclosing ellipse region) before being confirmed visually.

**A defect in `GlyphLab.tsx` itself, found and fixed while using it.** The lab's first draft applied the `is-focusing` class to the `<button>` element; the real `DomainNode.tsx` (and the CSS rules `.domain-node.is-focusing .domain-button`/`.domain-node.is-focusing .domain-node-glyph`) require it on the *wrapping* `.domain-node` element — the lab's "selected" toggle silently did nothing until this was corrected. A second, unrelated finding while debugging the toggle: `element.click()` dispatched from injected page-context JavaScript did not reliably reach this app's React event handlers in this browser-automation environment, while a genuine simulated mouse click did — a tooling quirk of the verification environment, not an application behavior, noted here so a future session doesn't waste time on the same false lead.

**Verification**: full frontend suite 252/252 (unchanged from D92 — no test needed new assertions, since `DomainGlyph.test.tsx`'s existing structural checks — distinct signature, `currentColor`-only, decorative, `variant` never renders fewer shapes, both variants share the same viewBox and first shape — already cover geometry changes without needing per-shape updates), `tsc -b`, `vite build`, and `oxlint` (13 warnings, all pre-existing categories, zero new — one new warning, an unused `variant` destructure in `PathPaths` after removing its last conditional use, was found and fixed by renaming to `_variant` before this count was taken). Live-verified in the temporary in-app comparison surface (all six glyphs, both variants, resting/hover/selected states, on the real dark HUD background) and then again in the real, unmodified app: canonical numbering unchanged and consistent everywhere; the Home→MIND transition and its header emblem confirmed correct; zero console errors. **Not independently re-confirmed this pass, same limitation as D91/D92**: the exact 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoints — `resize_window` again did not change the tab's actual rendered viewport in this session; at the actual rendered viewport, no horizontal overflow was present, and this pass changed only glyph path data (no layout, sizing-mechanism, or container change), so new overflow at other viewports is implausible but not directly observed. No backend, data, credential, schedule, OAuth, routine, or Hermes configuration was touched; no Git operation was performed.

## D94 — Domain icons replaced with the canonical `lucide-react` set (Activity/Boxes/CalendarDays/Brain/Compass/UsersRound), ending three rounds of hand-drawn iteration

**Context**: after three rounds of hand-drawn inline SVG (D91, D92, D93) — each fixing real problems the previous round introduced, but never quite reaching a result Bernardo considered finished — he supplied a second reference image and made a final decision: stop iterating on hand-drawn shapes entirely and adopt six specific, named icons from the official `lucide-react` package as **canonical**, with an explicit instruction that future sessions must not redraw or reinterpret them. This is a deliberate reversal of D91's original "never an external icon library" rule, made by explicit user decision, not a quiet architecture drift — CLAUDE.md's domain-iconography section has been rewritten in place to record the new rule as canonical rather than layering a contradiction on top of the old one.

**The canonical mapping** (`frontend/src/domainOrder.ts`'s numbering unchanged throughout): BODY → `Activity`, BUILD → `Boxes`, LIFE → `CalendarDays`, MIND → `Brain`, PATH → `Compass`, PEOPLE → `UsersRound`.

**Dependency**: `lucide-react` (`^1.37.0`) added as the one new dependency this task authorized, via `npm install lucide-react`. Only these six icons are imported by name (`import { Activity, Boxes, Brain, CalendarDays, Compass, UsersRound } from "lucide-react"`) — never a barrel or wildcard import — so tree-shaking keeps the rest of Lucide's icon set out of the production bundle. Confirmed empirically, not just assumed: the production JS bundle grew by only ~1KB gzipped (98.57 KB → 99.53 KB) despite the package containing well over a thousand icons; Vite's build-time "modules transformed" count rose sharply (73 → 1874) purely because Lucide ships one small `.mjs` file per icon that Vite's transform pipeline touches while resolving the six real imports — a dev/build-time artifact, not evidence against tree-shaking, which operates at the bundler's output stage and was confirmed by the actual shipped size.

**`DomainGlyph.tsx` rewritten, not layered on top of the old one.** The entire hand-drawn path-data implementation (all six `*Paths` functions, the `GlyphPathsProps`/`DomainGlyphVariant` types, the `variant="home"|"header"` conditional-detail mechanism) was deleted outright rather than kept behind a flag — Lucide icons are complete, pre-optimized vectors at every size, so there is no "secondary detail to hide at small size" concept left to serve. The component's public contract simplifies to `{ slug, className }`; internally it looks up the icon in a `Record<DomainSlug, LucideIcon>` and renders it with `strokeWidth={1.75}` (one shared value, in the icon's own 24×24 viewBox units — since CSS, not this component, controls the rendered pixel size via the same `.domain-node-glyph`/`.domain-glyph` classes as before, viewBox-relative stroke width already scales proportionally with whatever size CSS assigns, which is what keeps every icon equally weighted rather than needing per-icon size tuning) and `absoluteStrokeWidth` (Lucide's own explicit mechanism for the same guarantee, kept as a second, independent safeguard). `DomainNode.tsx` and `DomainEmblem.tsx` were updated to drop their now-meaningless `variant="home"`/`variant="header"` props — no other call site, and no CSS, needed to change, since sizing was always driven by the shared class names rather than the component's internal implementation.

**Nothing about the surrounding system changed.** `frontend/src/domainOrder.ts` (canonical numbering), `transitions/domainViewTransition.ts` (the shared-element morph — still keyed on `data-domain-transition-slug`, indifferent to what's rendered inside the tagged element), Home's layout, DomainView's layout, the violet/cyan/hover/focus/`is-focusing` color states, and every accessibility guarantee (decorative icon, real accessible name on the enclosing control) all carry over unchanged, confirmed by re-running the full existing test surface from D91-D93 without modification.

**A temporary comparison pass was judged unnecessary and not built this time** — unlike D91-D93's hand-drawn attempts, where subtle geometry choices repeatedly produced unintended readings only visible once rendered, these are six extremely standard, pre-vetted, widely-recognized official icons (a pulse line, stacked boxes, a calendar grid, a brain, a compass, two rounded figures) with no comparable ambiguity risk. Verification was instead performed directly in the real, unmodified app (Home and a real domain header, both variants, hover state, console, overflow) — a stronger signal than a synthetic comparison surface would have added for this specific, low-ambiguity change. All six were confirmed immediately unambiguous on sight, including specifically ruling out every rejected reading Bernardo listed (BUILD does not read as an org chart; PATH does not read as a bone/wand; MIND is unmistakably a brain; PEOPLE does not read as headphones or a moustache; LIFE does not read as an umbrella/crosshair/lock).

**New/rewritten tests**: `components/DomainGlyph.test.tsx` was rewritten for the new architecture (8 tests: exact icon per domain via Lucide's own `lucide-<kebab-name>` CSS class, no two domains share an icon, `currentColor`/`fill="none"`, no letter fallback or embedded fill shape, decorative/non-duplicating accessible name, unrecognized-slug fallback, shared `.domain-glyph` class plus caller className, one consistent stroke width across all six). A new `DomainGlyph.integration.test.tsx` (3 tests) directly proves the three cross-cutting guarantees D94 was asked to prove structurally rather than by inspection alone: Home's node and that domain's own header render the identical Lucide icon class through the identical component; the icon is `aria-hidden` while the enclosing button's own `aria-label` still carries the real domain name/description; and a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` stub leaves the icon and the domain's name fully present (reduced motion is a purely visual/animation concern, never an identity/content concern).

**Verification**: full frontend suite 255/255 (11 new: 8 in the rewritten `DomainGlyph.test.tsx`, 3 in the new integration file); `tsc -b`, `vite build` (bundle growth ~1KB gzipped, confirming tree-shaking), and `oxlint` (13 warnings, all pre-existing categories, zero new — the transient `_variant` unused-parameter warning from D93 is gone entirely now that the whole variant mechanism was deleted) all pass. Live-verified in the real, unmodified production build: correct icon and correct canonical number on every Home node and every domain header; the Home→BUILD transition and header emblem confirmed carrying the identical `Boxes` icon; violet hover state confirmed live; zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at the actual rendered viewport. **Not independently re-confirmed this pass, same limitation as D91-D93**: the exact 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoints specifically, for the same `resize_window` tool limitation already on record. No backend, data, credential, schedule, OAuth, routine, or Hermes configuration was touched; no Git operation was performed.

## D95 — Mission Control / Current Focus: a single-active-session focus timer built on Phase 12A-12C's shared briefing/Mission-Focus machinery, never a second task system

**Context**: Bernardo asked for a "what should I do right now" experience with a real, persisted, timed focus session — start/pause/resume/complete/abandon a single "Current Mission" at a time, reachable from Home, a domain page, the command palette, or voice, with candidates drawn from the same deterministic assembler Phase 12A already built. Explicitly not a general task manager, Kanban board, autonomous agent, or second briefing engine — a small, bounded extension layered on existing machinery, in the same spirit as Phase 12C's Mission Focus watchlist. Internally referred to during implementation as "Phase 12D... no — Mission Control" (see migration 0015's own docstring): it deliberately does not claim a new phase number, since nothing about it changes the shape of Phases 1-11 or 12A-12C, it just adds one bounded capability on top.

**Candidates reuse, never re-rank**: `app/mission_control_service.py`'s `mission_candidates()` takes an already-assembled `HomeBriefing` (from `app.briefing_service.assemble_home_briefing()`, called exactly once by the router — `GET /api/mission-control/candidates` reuses the existing `trigger="home_view"` value from migration 0013's fixed `BRIEFING_SNAPSHOT_TRIGGERS` CHECK constraint, since editing an already-head migration to add a dedicated trigger value was out of scope and unnecessary — this call shares Home's own ledger/snapshot trail exactly, so it can never disagree with what Home itself shows) and only partitions its already-sorted NOW/NEXT/WATCH items into one recommended candidate, up to two alternatives, and the WATCH set shown separately. Every candidate is phrased to the client as "Suggested from current information" (enforced by a docstring on `MissionCandidatesRead` and honored by the frontend's copy) — never as a definitive claim about what matters most. Source resolution for "start from a real item" reuses Phase 12C's `resolve_pin_source()` verbatim, for the same five eligible source types plus a sixth, `manual`, for free text with an explicit domain choice.

**Schema** (migration `0015`, forward-only, `0001`-`0014` untouched): one new table, `focus_sessions` — `title`, `domain_id` (nullable, resolved server-side), `source_type`/`source_id` (typed pointer, `manual` has no `source_id`), `source_title_snapshot` (frozen display fallback), `status` (`planned`/`active`/`paused`/`completed`/`abandoned`), `target_duration_minutes` (CHECK 5-180), `started_at`/`paused_at`/`accumulated_paused_seconds`/`completed_at`, `completion_note`/`what_changed_note`/`abandoned_reason`. **At most one row may be `active`/`paused` at a time, enforced at the database level** by a partial unique index on a constant expression — `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_focus_sessions_one_in_flight ON focus_sessions((1)) WHERE status IN ('active', 'paused')` — verified directly against a second, independent raw `sqlite3` connection bypassing the service layer entirely (both a duplicate INSERT and an illegal UPDATE are refused). Simpler than Phase 12C's trigger-based 5-pin enforcement, since this is a singleton constraint rather than a bounded-count one.

**Timer is always derived state, never a stored countdown**: `elapsed_seconds(row, now)` computes purely from `started_at`, `accumulated_paused_seconds`, and either `now` (while active), `paused_at` (while paused), or `completed_at` (once terminal) — an ordinary backend/frontend restart is accurate for free, with no persisted "time remaining" value that could drift or need reconciling. Pausing folds the just-elapsed paused window into `accumulated_paused_seconds` on resume; completing or abandoning directly from `paused` folds the final open pause window first (`_fold_pause_if_any`), so that window is never miscounted as focus time. Every lifecycle transition (`pause_mission`/`resume_mission`/`complete_mission`/`abandon_mission`) is idempotent by design — a duplicate/retried call on a session already in the target state returns it unchanged rather than erroring, satisfying the reliability-audit-style "duplicate request" requirement as a design property, not an afterthought.

**A real defect found and fixed during this pass**: `pause_mission()`'s first draft stamped `paused_at` but never actually set `row.status = "paused"` — a session paused this way would have kept reporting `status="active"` forever, silently breaking resume, breaking the "only one active/paused session" UI logic that reads status (though not the database-level constraint itself, which correctly keys on the real underlying status column), and breaking every elapsed-time calculation that branches on status. Caught by a service-level test asserting `row.status == "paused"` after the call, not by inspection. A second defect: the router's initial `assemble_home_briefing(..., trigger="mission_control")` call violated migration 0013's `ck_briefing_snapshots_trigger_valid` CHECK constraint (a fixed enum with no such value) — every `/api/mission-control/candidates` call failed with a raw `IntegrityError` until switched to the existing `trigger="home_view"` value (see above). A third: an existing test, `test_migration_0014.py::test_full_fresh_migration_chain_reaches_0014`, hardcoded `"0014"` as the expected head revision — updated to compare against `get_head_revision()` instead of a literal, since forward-only migrations after 0014 (this one, and any future one) are an expected, not exceptional, condition.

**Restore safety mirrors Phase 8/9/10's established pattern, with correct pause-time folding**: `app/import_service.py`'s `_interrupt_active_focus_sessions()` — called from `restore_archive()` right alongside `_expire_stale_action_proposals`/`_disconnect_all_integrations`/`_disable_all_integration_schedules`/`_disable_all_routine_schedules` — forces any `active`/`paused` row to `abandoned` with `abandoned_reason='restored_into_installation'` on **every** restore (the same installation or a genuinely different one), since an export/restore is never allowed to let a focus session silently keep running against a base state that no longer reflects live reality. An **ordinary restart on the same installation is deliberately untouched** by this function — it only ever runs from inside `restore_archive()`, never from `main.py`'s startup path, so a session survives a normal backend restart exactly as Phase 12C's pins already do. Unlike the raw-SQL date-string-arithmetic approach that would have been fragile across the ISO-with-timezone vs. SQLAlchemy's own naive-string datetime formats, the function fetches matching rows, computes any final open-pause-window duration in Python (parsing the one format this codebase itself ever writes), and writes back a single corrected `accumulated_paused_seconds` — so a session interrupted while paused doesn't have that paused window miscounted as focus time in its historical record.

**Privacy**: identical structural guarantee to Phase 12A-12C — `assemble_home_briefing()` never reads a MIND/PEOPLE source regardless of settings flags, and `mission_candidates()` only ever partitions what that function already returned, so MIND/PEOPLE content cannot reach a candidate through Mission Control by any path. A `manual` mission may be assigned to any domain, including MIND/PEOPLE, since that is Bernardo's own explicit, direct action, not something Jarvis surfaced or suggested. No mission data is ever sent to Hermes/a model except through the existing general-conversation turn endpoint, and only when Bernardo explicitly invokes it — nothing in `mission_control_service.py` or its router imports a provider module or calls `send_turn`, confirmed by an AST-walk test rather than by inspection alone (mirroring Phase 12C's equivalent test).

**Backend API** (`app/routers/mission_control.py`): `GET /api/mission-control/candidates`, `GET /api/mission-control/current`, `GET /api/mission-control/history`, `POST /api/mission-control/sessions`, `POST .../{id}/pause`, `.../resume`, `.../complete`, `.../abandon` — every mutation is a direct local action (CLAUDE.md §12 read/direct tier), never the Phase 8 propose→approve→execute lifecycle, since nothing here ever touches Calendar/memory/Health/an external system, and ending a session never mutates the source it was started from (verified directly: completing a mission started from a real LIFE task leaves that task's payload byte-for-byte unchanged).

**Frontend**: `api.ts` extended with the full typed client plus `computeElapsedSeconds`/`computeRemainingSeconds` (a TypeScript re-derivation of the backend's own formula, so the UI's live-ticking display and the backend's authoritative value can never structurally diverge — a `setInterval` only ever triggers a re-render, never holds or decrements the real value itself). `commands/registry.ts` extended with seven new safe, local, never-confirm-required commands (`focus_start` — pattern-matched with an optional spoken duration, e.g. "focus on this for 45 minutes"; `focus_pause`/`focus_resume`/`focus_complete`/`focus_abandon` — keyword-presence matched and routed through the exact same negation/question (`explanatoryOnly`) gate every other safe action already uses, so "don't pause this" or "what happens if I finish this?" correctly do not execute; `focus_show_current`/`focus_show_history` — exact-alias reads that deliberately bypass the interrogative half of that gate, since their own natural phrasing is a question — "What did I accomplish today?" — that Bernardo's own spec names as a command to execute, not an explanation to give). New `components/MissionControlStrip.tsx` (the Home surface, in Home's cinematic register per CLAUDE.md §20, not the internal-console register) and `components/FocusOnThisButton.tsx` (a domain-page entry point mirroring `AddToMissionFocusButton`'s existing pattern). `components/JarvisCore.tsx` gained a `focusActive` prop that is deliberately **not** part of `CoreState` — it only ever appends a modifier class alongside `state-idle`, so it structurally cannot coexist with, and can never visually outrank, the listening/transcribing/thinking/speaking/error states, which each already carry their own real `state` value.

**A real defect found in existing (non-Mission-Control) code during this pass**: `App.tsx`'s `resolveCommand` never handled the `safe_action` result kind at all — typing a safe-action phrase like "sync calendar" or "run morning briefing" into the Command Palette silently did nothing, since the palette path only ever recognized `navigate`/`confirm_required`, even though voice already executed these same phrases correctly through a different code path. Fixed as a small, generic addition (not scoped to Mission Control), verified against the existing `App.test.tsx`/`App.shortcuts.test.tsx` suite plus the new command-registry tests.

**Deliberate scope decisions**: item 7d's domain-page entry point was implemented (`FocusOnThisButton` on eligible LIFE/PATH/BUILD records, identical eligibility to `AddToMissionFocusButton`), not skipped. No new global keyboard shortcut was added — CLAUDE.md's existing fixed shortcut list (Control+Option+J, Space, Escape, 1-6, Command+K, Command+Shift+E) is treated as closed, not casually extended; the command palette, voice, and on-screen controls are Mission Control's only entry points beyond a Home candidate click.

**Verification**: full backend suite 671/671 (59 new: `test_mission_control_service.py` 36, `test_mission_control_api.py` 13, `test_mission_control_export_restore.py` 5, `test_mission_control_privacy.py` 5, plus a corrected `test_migration_0014.py` assertion); full frontend suite 325/325 (34 in three new dedicated files — `MissionControlStrip.test.tsx` 20, `JarvisCore.test.tsx` 7, `Home.missionControl.test.tsx` 7 — plus an unverified additional count folded into the existing `registry.test.ts`, now 124 cases total; no pre-change baseline was captured for that file, so the exact number of new cases within it isn't independently confirmed here); `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `ruff check` (backend, clean), and `oxlint` (frontend; zero new warning categories, all remaining warnings pre-existing `set-state-in-effect`/`exhaustive-deps` patterns already present identically across most other view files) all pass. **Not done this pass**: a real dev-server/browser visual check of the new Home strip and active-mission state, and a real Bernardo's-Mac acceptance pass against his actual `~/JarvisData` — both explicitly deferred exactly like every other Phase 12 sub-phase before real-Mac acceptance is separately authorized.

**Boundary respected**: no routine enabled, changed, or rescheduled; no Hermes toolset, OAuth, credential, or real provider account touched; no sub-agent/cron/email/notification/browser-automation/terminal/filesystem capability introduced; the Home orbit and canonical domain glyphs were not redesigned; no Phase 12D (a genuinely new phase) was started, only this bounded extension; no Git operation was performed; all implementation and testing used fictional fixtures, an injected/fake clock, and the existing fake-Hermes-CLI test harness — Bernardo's real Calendar, Health account, Keychain, routines, schedules, and personal data were never touched.

## D96 — Mission Control real-Mac acceptance: migration 0015 applied live, two real defects found and fixed against real data, one pre-existing defect found and deliberately left unfixed

**Context**: D95's implementation and container verification were complete but explicitly deferred real-Mac acceptance. This entry records that acceptance pass, run against Bernardo's actual `~/JarvisData` on his real Mac (Darwin arm64), using the normal `scripts/jarvisctl.sh` flow throughout — never a manual Alembic invocation, never a direct database edit outside the app's own code paths.

**Safety before migration**: a real backup (`POST /api/backups`) and a real export (`POST /api/export`) were taken and both validated — the export's own checksum matched its manifest exactly, and the backup file's SHA-256 matched its sidecar `.sha256` file exactly. Non-secret row counts were recorded across every table (`domains`=6, `conversations`=3, `messages`=4, `domain_summaries`=6, `calendar_calendars`=4, `calendar_event_cache`=1, `google_health_daily_summaries`=35, `google_health_sessions`=27, `action_proposals`=2, `action_audit_events`=8, `hook_events`=9, `skills`/`skill_versions`=4, `briefing_snapshots`=37, `integration_connections`=2, `integration_sync_schedules`=2, `integration_sync_runs`=87, `routine_schedules`=3, `routine_runs`=0, `agent_runs`=2, `context_snapshots`=2). Both integrations confirmed `connected` with only read-only Calendar/Health scopes (no write scope present); both sync schedules confirmed `enabled`; all three routine schedules confirmed `enabled=false` (unchanged — this pass never enables a routine); the `jarvis` Hermes profile confirmed present and its gateway running, with no config/toolset/credential inspected or touched beyond `hermes profile show` (no token/key/secret ever displayed). Pre-migration schema revision confirmed `0014` directly against `alembic_version` (read-only query, not an Alembic invocation).

**Migration applied live**: `./scripts/jarvisctl.sh stop` then `./scripts/jarvisctl.sh open` — `open`'s own `apply_migrations()` step ran `alembic upgrade head` internally exactly as it does on every ordinary start, never invoked directly by this session. Post-migration: `alembic_version` reads `0015`; `focus_sessions` (with its `uq_focus_sessions_one_in_flight` partial unique index) exists; exactly one logical backend process (the `uv run` wrapper plus its uvicorn child, the same process tree `jarvisctl.sh`'s own PID-verification logic already treats as one); the Hermes gateway confirmed bound to `127.0.0.1:8642` only; `GET /api/health` returns `ok`.

**Regression/data-integrity verification**: every row count above re-checked identically post-migration (the only change was `briefing_snapshots` 37→38, an expected append from the Home view itself loading — never a loss). Both integration schedules and all three routine schedules re-confirmed unchanged (routines still `enabled=false`). All six domains still present via `GET /api/domains`.

**Candidate verification with real data**: `GET /api/mission-control/candidates` genuinely returned an empty result (`recommended: null`), and this was confirmed truthful, not a bug — the one real cached Calendar event is 3 days out (outside the NOW/NEXT window), `structured_records` held zero LIFE/PATH/BUILD rows, and both real action proposals were already terminal (`succeeded`), matching `GET /api/briefing/home`'s own `items: []` exactly. A live AST check against the actual deployed `mission_control_service.py` confirmed (again, now against production code rather than only the test suite) no model/Hermes import, no `send_turn` call, and no internal call to `assemble_home_briefing()` — the router's one call remains the only assembly. A real manual mission explicitly assigned to the MIND domain was created and immediately abandoned to confirm that explicit path still works (never auto-surfaced, only reachable by Bernardo's own explicit domain choice) — this doubled as a real-data confirmation of the "manual + abandon never mutates anything" acceptance criterion, since a manual mission has no source to mutate.

**Real lifecycle acceptance**: a real focus session was started directly against the real cached Calendar event's real ID (`source_type=calendar_event`, resolved regardless of the NOW/NEXT/WATCH window, exactly as `resolve_pin_source()` is designed to work) — domain resolved correctly to `life`, source title snapshot correct. The database-level singleton constraint was proven live (a second concurrent start attempt returned HTTP 400). Real wall-clock timing was exercised end-to-end: elapsed advanced correctly while active, froze exactly during a real ~6-second pause, and resumed counting from the frozen value rather than including the paused gap — verified via both the API and the real browser UI, which stayed in agreement throughout a Pause→Resume→Complete cycle performed through actual UI clicks. Completion accepted a real note. `GET /api/mission-control/current` returned `null` immediately after, and the underlying `calendar_event_cache` row was confirmed byte-for-byte identical (`all_day`, `start_datetime` unchanged) before and after. A domain-page entry point (`FocusOnThisButton` on a real, clearly-labelled `[ACCEPTANCE TEST]` LIFE task created for this purpose) was exercised and correctly resolved the real `life_task` source and `life` domain; the test session was abandoned and the test record archived (not deleted) immediately afterward, leaving no dangling state.

**Restart continuity**: a real active manual mission survived a genuine `jarvisctl.sh stop`/`open` cycle with the exact same session id, correctly advanced elapsed time across the real downtime, produced no duplicate row, and triggered no integration-sync or routine run (`integration_sync_runs`/`routine_runs` counts unchanged across the restart) — the real Home HUD correctly re-rendered the truthful restored ACTIVE state after reload.

**Voice/command safety** (tested via the command palette against the real backend, as text — no working microphone was available in this environment, and this is explicitly noted rather than silently substituted; the palette and voice paths share the exact same `parseCommand()` — see below): "start a focus session for 25/10/5 minutes" correctly started a real session each time; "Pause the current mission" / "Resume the current mission" / "Finish the current focus session" all executed correctly against the real active session, each confirmed via both the toast feedback and the live-refreshed strip; "What happens if I finish this?" and "Don't finish this mission" both correctly returned "No matching commands" (refused, session left untouched) — the interrogative and negation gates confirmed live, not just in the unit suite; "Finish the current focus session" was confirmed to touch only the focus session itself (`action_proposals`, `calendar_event_cache`, and `structured_records` all confirmed unchanged immediately after). **On "What should I do now?"**: this phrase does not match any Mission Control command alias (by design — it is deliberately not hard-coded as one, since it is an open-ended question rather than a fixed control phrase) and instead falls through to `parseCommand`'s `"none"` result, which `App.tsx`'s pre-existing (Phase 6, not part of this feature) ambient-conversation mechanism routes to a real model call via `sendTurn`. This routing was verified by source inspection plus the already-passing `App.globalVoice.test.tsx` coverage of that exact fallback (a mocked equivalent question, asserted to call `sendTurn` with an empty domain array) rather than by firing a live model call — judged unnecessary since the routing correctness was already conclusively established without spending a real Hermes/model call for zero additional signal. **Net model calls made during this entire acceptance pass: zero.**

**Two real defects found and fixed live against real data**:
1. **Home's Mission Control strip did not refresh immediately after a command-palette/voice focus action.** Starting/pausing/resuming/completing a mission from `App.tsx`'s `executeSafeAction` correctly mutated the backend but never notified `Home.tsx`'s already-mounted poll loop, so the strip kept showing its stale prior state until the next 30-second poll or a reload — confirmed live (toast said "Started focus…" while the strip still read "No suggested focus candidates right now") before being traced to its root cause and fixed. Fix: a new `MISSION_CONTROL_REFRESH_EVENT` (`api.ts`) dispatched on `window` immediately after each of the five mutating actions succeeds; `Home.tsx` adds a listener for it alongside its existing `"focus"` listener, calling the exact same `refresh()` function — no new refetch mechanism, just an additional trigger for the one that already existed. Verified live after rebuilding: the strip now updates instantly for both the command-palette and keyboard-only-form paths. One new regression test (`Home.missionControl.test.tsx`).
2. **Starting a mission from the manual keyboard-only entry form dropped focus to `<body>`.** Confirmed live during a keyboard-only walkthrough of the "Start a custom focus session" form: after activating "Start focus session" via Enter, `document.activeElement` was `<body>` — React unmounting the form and mounting `ActiveMission` in its place silently lost focus entirely, a real WCAG 2.4.3 (Focus Order) gap a screen-reader or keyboard-only user would hit on every single mission start. Fix: `ActiveMission` (`MissionControlStrip.tsx`) gained a `tabIndex={-1}` ref on its own title heading, focused via a `useEffect` keyed on `session.id` (so it fires once per new session, never on an ordinary status/timer re-render of the same session). Verified live both via the keyboard-only form path and the command-palette path — `document.activeElement` correctly lands on the mission title in both cases. Two new regression tests (`MissionControlStrip.test.tsx`): focus lands on mount, and does not get stolen back on an ordinary re-render.

**One pre-existing defect found and deliberately left unfixed**: an intermittent "Unhandled Rejection" (`window is not defined`, `ReferenceError`) surfaces non-deterministically (roughly 1 run in 3) during the full frontend suite, traced to `Home.tsx`'s original `loadCurrentMission`/`loadBriefing` callbacks calling `setState` from a `.then`/`.catch` with no unmount guard — a promise that settles after a test's jsdom environment has already been torn down. This predates and is unrelated to both fixes above (the traceback points at `loadCurrentMission`'s original `.catch()` block, and the identical unguarded pattern already exists in `loadBriefing`, written before Mission Control). It never causes an actual test assertion to fail, only a vitest-level warning, and has no realistic live-browser consequence (a real fetch essentially never fails at the exact instant a real user navigates away). Fixing it properly would mean auditing and adding an unmount-guard (the `cancelled`-flag pattern `Home.tsx`'s own `fetchDomains` effect already uses) to every async loader in `Home.tsx`, which is broader than this bounded Mission Control acceptance pass and edges toward the "do not redesign the Home HUD" boundary — left as a known, narrow, low-priority follow-up rather than fixed here.

**Export/restore drill**: a real, clearly-labelled temporary active mission was created, then a real export of the live installation was taken and validated (`schema_revision: "0015"`, `secrets_included: false`). It was restored into a brand-new, fully isolated destination directory (never `~/JarvisData`) via `restore_archive()` called directly. In the restored copy: the temporary mission was correctly `abandoned` with `abandoned_reason="restored_into_installation"`; the fold-in-Python pause-time-correction logic ran without needing a pause window this time (none was open) and the row's `paused_at` was correctly `NULL`; mission history fully survived (7 abandoned + 2 completed rows from this session's various tests, all present); zero `active`/`paused` rows remained; both integrations were `disconnected`; both integration sync schedules and all three routine schedules were `disabled`; the restored database accepted a real write (a throwaway table create/insert/drop). The real, live installation was independently re-confirmed completely unaffected immediately afterward — same test mission still genuinely `active`, both integrations still `connected`, the Calendar sync schedule still `enabled`.

**Visual/accessibility**: `MISSION_CONTROL_REFRESH_EVENT`'s new active-state CSS (`.mc-active`, `.mc-duration-picker`, `.mc-domain-picker`, `.jarvis-hud.state-idle.is-focus-active`) was confirmed by source inspection to introduce zero new `animation`/`transition` rules — `.is-focus-active` is a bare `--ring-color`/`--ring-glow` custom-property override layered onto the pre-existing idle ring animation, which is already gated by index.css's established `prefers-reduced-motion` rule, so there is nothing new here requiring its own guard. `focusActive`'s class was confirmed, live in the DOM, to apply only alongside `state-idle` (`jarvis-hud state-idle is-focus-active`), never alongside a real voice state. Keyboard-only operation was walked through live end-to-end (tab into the manual-entry form, select a domain via `role="radio"`/`aria-checked`, select a duration via `aria-pressed`, submit, then Tab through Pause/Complete/Abandon) with a visible focus ring confirmed at every step. Status is always a real text label (`ACTIVE`/`PAUSED`), never color alone. The live timer display carries `aria-live="off"`, with a separate visually-hidden region announcing only once per elapsed minute (`aria-live="polite"`) — confirmed in source, matching the design's own stated intent to avoid a per-second screen-reader announcement. Domain glyphs and the canonical 1-6 numbering were confirmed unchanged and correctly ordered live on Home (BODY=1, BUILD=2, LIFE=3, MIND=4, PATH=5, PEOPLE=6). **Not achievable this pass**: a genuine 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 resize — `resize_window` was confirmed, live, to not actually change `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` in this environment (a request for 1024×768 left the real viewport at 1440×757), the same limitation already on record from D91-D94. A best-effort proxy (constraining the `#root` container to 820px width directly via a temporary style override) showed no internal overflow, but this exercises the layout's own flex/wrap behavior only, not real `@media` breakpoints keyed on actual viewport width, and is reported as a weak proxy rather than genuine confirmation.

**Verification**: no full-suite rerun was skipped, since two real defects were found and fixed — full backend suite re-run once, 671/671; full frontend suite re-run, 328/328 (net +3 from the two new regression-test additions above), `tsc -b` clean, `oxlint` clean (only pre-existing warning categories), `vite build` clean, all after the fixes.

**Boundary respected**: no routine was enabled, changed, or rescheduled at any point (all three confirmed `enabled=false` throughout, including post-restore in the isolated copy); no OAuth flow, Keychain entry, credential, or Hermes profile/toolset configuration was touched (only read via `hermes profile show`, no secret ever displayed); no Calendar or Health data was mutated (the one real Calendar event used for testing was confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged); every test artifact created (`[ACCEPTANCE TEST]` sessions and the one LIFE task record) was cleaned up immediately (abandoned/archived, never deleted, matching the system's own tombstone discipline) except where the fixture itself required no cleanup (manual sessions with no source); `~/JarvisData` was never restored over; no Phase 12D work was started; no Git operation was performed at any point in this pass.

## D97 — Phase 12C acceptance reconciliation: integration-scope regression check (none found), lingering-test-data check (none found), and a wording correction to D96's status summary (not D96 itself)

**Context**: before starting Phase 12D, Bernardo asked for a narrow reconciliation pass — confirm real Google Calendar/Health scopes show no regression, confirm no acceptance-test artifact from D96 was left lingering, and correct any place where D96's two explicitly-deferred checks (a real microphone voice test, genuine 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoint inspection) had been summarized elsewhere as if they'd passed. No product code was touched in this pass — inspection and documentation only, per explicit instruction.

**Integration permissions verified, no regression**: `GET /api/integrations` against the real running backend returned, for `google_calendar`: `calendar.events.readonly`, `calendar.events.owned`, `calendar.calendarlist.readonly` — all three expected scopes present, including `calendar.events.owned` (the one whose absence was to be treated as an immediate-stop regression; it is present). For `google_health`: `googlehealth.health_metrics_and_measurements.readonly`, `googlehealth.sleep.readonly`, `googlehealth.activity_and_fitness.readonly` — all three expected scopes present. No credential, token, or secret was displayed or requested; no disconnect, revoke, reconnect, OAuth consent, or scope-metadata mutation was performed.

**No lingering acceptance-test state**: `focus_sessions` holds 7 `abandoned` + 2 `completed` rows and zero `active`/`paused`/`planned` rows; `GET /api/mission-control/current` confirms `null`; `GET /api/mission-control/history` returns exactly those 9 terminal rows. The one temporary LIFE task created during D96 remains archived (tombstoned, not deleted, per the system's own retention discipline) and is correctly excluded from `GET /api/domains/life/records` (0 live rows) — no un-archived temporary record remains anywhere.

**Wording correction (this is a correction to living status documents, not to D96 itself — D96's own body already carefully disclosed both deferred items; the overclaiming was introduced only in the shorter summary lines of `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `README.md`, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §18, which is what this entry corrects)**: those three documents previously used unqualified phrases like "real-Mac acceptance complete" / "**real-Mac accepted**" for Mission Control as a whole. They now explicitly read that lifecycle, persistence, privacy, restart continuity, export/restore, command-parser safety, and source immutability are real-Mac accepted, while two items remain **explicitly not completed** and are folded into Phase 6's own pre-existing outstanding real-hardware/visual acceptance record (the same one tracked since D72/D77/D78/D79/D91 above) rather than treated as a new, Mission-Control-specific gap: (1) a real microphone voice-command test — D96's command-palette testing exercised the identical `parseCommand()` logic voice also uses, which is real evidence the parsing/negation/interrogative-gating logic is correct, but is not itself a test of real speech input; (2) genuine 1280×800/1024×768/820×900 breakpoint inspection — confirmed again in this pass that `resize_window` does not actually change `window.innerWidth`/`window.innerHeight` in this environment, the same tool limitation on record since D91.

**Files changed (documentation only)**: `docs/ROADMAP.md` (Mission Control section heading and status paragraph), `README.md` (Mission Control status line), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §18 (verification paragraph). `docs/PRODUCT_SPEC.md`, `docs/DESIGN_DIRECTION.md`, and `CLAUDE.md` were reviewed and found already accurate — none made an acceptance-status claim that needed correcting, so none were changed.

**Tests**: none run. This was a documentation-only reconciliation; no implementation defect was found, so per the standing rule ("if this is documentation-only, do not rerun the full suites"), the full backend/frontend suites were deliberately not re-run. The two read-only checks above (integration scopes, lingering test data) were performed directly against the live real backend/database, not via the automated test suites.

**Boundary respected**: no OAuth or credential change; no provider mutation; no routine or schedule change; no Hermes configuration/toolset change; no model call of any kind; no Phase 12D implementation was started; no Git operation was performed.

## D98 — Phase 12D: Unified Recall and Provenance — deterministic local search across every domain, reusing FTS5 wherever it already existed

**Context**: Bernardo asked for one fast, trustworthy place to search everything Jarvis has stored locally across all six domains and open the exact underlying source — explicitly deterministic local retrieval, never a model feature (no Hermes call, no summary generation, no fact inference, no command execution, no treating retrieved text as an instruction, no matter what that text says). See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §19 for the durable architectural record this entry supports.

**Indexing strategy, chosen only after inspecting every write path**: a hybrid, not a first-choice default. Memory items keep using the pre-existing `memory_fts` (Phase 4) unchanged; document chunk text keeps using the pre-existing `document_fts` (Phase 9) unchanged; every other source type (conversations, messages, structured records, domain summaries, document names, cached Calendar events, action proposals, routine run outputs, Mission Control sessions) indexes into one new table, `recall_fts` (migration `0016`, forward-only, `0001`-`0015` untouched). `app/recall_service.search()` queries all three families and merges/ranks the results — the alternative of duplicating already-indexed content into a fourth copy was rejected specifically because it would recreate the exact "index drift" risk the phase was asked to prevent.

**Deliberately excluded sources, with reasons recorded rather than left implicit**: Google Health metrics/sessions (purely numeric telemetry with no natural free-text value — the product spec explicitly permits omitting this); `BriefingSnapshot` (stores only a digest/count, no real text at all); `BriefingItemState` (its title/subtitle always duplicate an already-independently-indexed source — indexing it would add write-path cost on a very hot path, Home's own view load, for zero new information — the exact case the spec's "where they add information not already represented by their source" clause exists to exclude); per-event `ActionAuditEvent` detail rows (the parent proposal's own `reason`/`expected_effect`/`error_summary` already carry the meaningful text); Mission Focus pins and `Skill`/`SkillVersion` (neither named in the product spec's own source list).

**Privacy is structural**: `search()` defaults to `domain_slugs=("life", "path", "build")` when the caller passes none at all — BODY/MIND/PEOPLE can only ever appear in a result set that explicitly names them, identical to Phase 12A/12B/12C/Mission Control's rule for the Home briefing. An explicit-but-empty domain list is honored literally, never silently widened. Every `recall_fts` row stores a `domain_slug` (nullable — `NULL` is the documented "global/system" classification: a general conversation, a domain-less manual mission, a global action proposal, every routine run), resolved once at write time so no query-time join against `domains` is ever needed.

**Ranking is one deterministic pipeline, never embeddings, never a model**: (1) each FTS family's raw bm25 scores are min-max normalized independently into a 0-1 relevance value before combining, since raw bm25 isn't comparable across differently-shaped tables; (2) an exact title/phrase match adds a fixed `+0.5`; (3) an optional `current_domain` hint (set when Recall opens from inside a domain) adds `+0.1` to same-domain results; (4) a capped, bounded recency signal (`max +0.05`, 365-day half-life) can only ever break a near-tie; (5) a final, always-reproducible tie-break sorts by `(source_type, source_id)`. **Why no model or embeddings anywhere in this feature**: search must be instant, free, fully offline-capable, and independently auditable by reading one module — a model or embedding-similarity call can guarantee none of that (latency, cost, non-determinism, a live network dependency this local-first product's own mandate rules out for something this core to daily use). Confirmed structurally by an AST-walk test proving `recall_service.py` imports no provider module and calls no `send_turn`, mirroring the identical guarantee Mission Focus/Mission Control already established.
